the Minister of Transport, pursuant to sections 4.71[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#fn_2-30e_tc_9277){#fn_2-30e_tc_9277-ID0EOFAA} and
4.9[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^b^](#fn_2-30e_tc_9278){#fn_2-30e_tc_9278-ID0EMFAA},
paragraphs 7.6(1)(a)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^c^](#fn_2-30e_tc_9281){#fn_2-30e_tc_9281-ID0EKFAA} and
(b)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^c^](#fn_2-30e_tc_9281){#fn_2-30e_tc_9281-ID0EIFAA} and
section 7.7[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^d^](#fn_2-30e_tc_9280){#fn_2-30e_tc_9280-ID0EGFAA} of
the [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^e^](#fn_2-30e_tc_9282){#fn_2-30e_tc_9282-ID0EDFAA},
hereby makes the annexed [Canadian Aviation Security Regulations,
2012](/eng/regulations/SOR-2011-318).

## [Overview]{.HTitleText1} {#h-775312 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations overview

- **[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} These
  Regulations are the principal means of supplementing the legislative
  framework set out in sections 4.7 to 4.87 of the Act. They are
  designed to enhance preparedness for acts or attempted acts of
  unlawful interference with civil aviation and to facilitate the
  detection of, prevention of, response to and recovery from acts or
  attempted acts of unlawful interference with civil aviation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other instruments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} These Regulations are supplemented from time to time
  by other aviation security regulations and by security measures,
  interim orders, ministerial orders and emergency directions.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-1-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Structure

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations are
divided into 14 parts: 

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} Part 1 deals with screening authorities and the
  screening of persons and goods at aerodromes;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} Part 2 deals with the CATSA security program;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} Part 3 deals with weapons, explosive substances and
  incendiary devices;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} Part 4 deals with security at aerodromes listed in
  Schedule 1;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} Part 5 deals with security at aerodromes listed in
  Schedule 2;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} Part 6 deals with security at aerodromes listed in
  Schedule 3 and at any other place designated by the Minister under
  subsection 6(1.1) of the [Canadian Air Transport Security Authority
  Act](/eng/acts/C-11.2);

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} Part 7 deals with security at other aerodromes;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} Part 8 deals with aircraft security;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} Part 9 is reserved;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} Part 10 is reserved;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} Part 11 deals with cargo and mail on flights
  departing from an aerodrome located in Canada;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} Part 12 deals with cargo on flights departing from a
  place outside Canada to an aerodrome located in Canada;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} Part 13 sets out ministerial powers and duties; and

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} Part 14 sets out an administrative monetary penalty
  scheme for the contravention of certain provisions of these
  Regulations and the provisions of any security measure.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-163, s. 1

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-775338 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in these Regulations.

[[accepted cargo]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[accepted cargo]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any cargo in respect of
    which an air waybill or other similar control document is
    issued. ([fret accepté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Aeronautics
    Act](/eng/acts/A-2). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[aerodrome security personnel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[aerodrome security personnel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    individuals who are employed by the operator of an aerodrome or by
    one of the operator's contractors to prepare for, detect, prevent,
    respond to, and assist in the recovery from, acts or attempted acts
    of unlawful interference with civil aviation. ([personnel de sûreté
    de l'aérodrome]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[agency firearm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[agency firearm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    section 1 of the [Public Agents Firearms
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-98-203). ([arme à feu
    d'agence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[baggage make-up area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[baggage make-up area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area of an air
    terminal building, or an area of a building at an aerodrome that is
    under an air carrier's control, where checked baggage is
    stored. ([zone de tri de bagages]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[biometric template]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[biometric template]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a template
    generated by algorithms that encode an identifiable physiological or
    behavioural characteristic of a person. ([modèle
    biométrique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[border services officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[border services officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
    as in section 5 of the [Preclearance Act,
    2016](/eng/acts/P-19.32). ([agent des services
    frontaliers]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[carry-on baggage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[carry-on baggage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any baggage and
    personal belongings to which a person has or will have access on
    board an aircraft. ([bagages de cabine]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[CATSA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[CATSA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Air Transport
    Security Authority established under subsection 5(1) of the
    [Canadian Air Transport Security Authority
    Act](/eng/acts/C-11.2). ([ACSTA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[checked baggage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[checked baggage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any baggage and
    personal belongings in respect of which a baggage tag is issued
    after the baggage and personal belongings are accepted for
    transportation. ([bagages enregistrés]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[combination code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[combination code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a series or
    combination of numbers, letters or other characters that is assigned
    to a person by, or under the authority of, the operator of an
    aerodrome and that, when entered into mechanical or electronic
    equipment on or near a door, gate or other device, unlocks or
    releases the door, gate or other device and allows access to a
    restricted area. ([code d'accès]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[consolidated cargo]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[consolidated cargo]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means multiple cargo
    shipments that originate from more than one original shipper that
    are consolidated to form one shipment under the air waybill or
    similar control document. ([fret groupé]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[crew member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[crew member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person assigned to duty
    on an aircraft during flight time by the operator of the
    aircraft. ([membre d'équipage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[defensive equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[defensive equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a baton or
    oleoresin capsicum spray, or both. ([équipement
    défensif]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[document of entitlement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[document of entitlement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a document
    that serves as proof of authorization to enter a restricted
    area. ([document d'autorisation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[escort officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[escort officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a peace officer; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person authorized by the federal government
      or a provincial government or any of their agencies to escort a
      person in custody on a flight. ([agent d'escorte]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[exigent circumstances]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[exigent circumstances]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means circumstances
    beyond a border services officer's or a US preclearance officer's
    control in which the officer's continued presence is required to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} preserve the integrity of an examination or
      investigation that is underway; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent an imminent danger to human life or
      serious damage to property. ([situation
      d'urgence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[firearm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[firearm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section 2
    of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46). ([arme à
    feu]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[general list of prohibited items]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[general list of prohibited items]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Part
    1 of TP 14628, which lists or describes goods that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} could pose a threat to aviation security;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are prohibited as carry-on baggage by the
      governments of other countries; or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are identified by the International Civil
      Aviation Organization as items that must never be carried in the
      cabin of an aircraft or taken into a restricted area. ([liste
      générale des articles interdits]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[incendiary device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[incendiary device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an object, other
    than a match or pocket lighter, that is fabricated with combustible
    materials and is designed to inflict burn injuries on individuals or
    to cause fire damage to property. ([engin
    incendiaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is authorized
    by the Minister to carry out an inspection under subsection 8.7(1)
    of the Act. ([inspecteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[key]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[key]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a key, card or other device,
    including a functionality that can be added to a restricted area
    identity card, that is designed to allow access to a restricted area
    and that is issued to an individual by, or under the authority of,
    the operator of an aerodrome. ([clé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
    Transport. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[non-consolidated cargo]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[non-consolidated cargo]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a cargo
    shipment that originates from a single shipper. ([fret non
    groupé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[non-passenger screening checkpoint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[non-passenger screening checkpoint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    restricted area access point or a location inside a restricted area
    where persons other than passengers are screened or can be
    screened. ([point de contrôle des non-passagers]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[operator of an aerodrome]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[operator of an aerodrome]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an aerodrome that is not an
      airport, the person in charge of the aerodrome, and includes an
      employee, an agent or a representative of the person in charge of
      the aerodrome;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an airport, the holder of the
      Canadian aviation document issued in respect of the airport or the
      person in charge of the airport, and includes an employee, an
      agent or a representative of the holder of the Canadian aviation
      document; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an aerodrome or part of an
      aerodrome that is operated by the Minister of National Defence and
      used by an air carrier, the person in charge of commercial air
      service operations at the aerodrome. ([exploitant d'un
      aérodrome]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[operator of an aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[operator of an aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the person who
    has possession of the aircraft as owner, lessee or
    otherwise. ([utilisateur d'un aéronef]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[passenger screening checkpoint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[passenger screening checkpoint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    screening checkpoint that is intended primarily for the screening of
    departing passengers. ([point de contrôle des
    passagers]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[peace officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[peace officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a member of the Correctional Service of Canada
      who is designated as a peace officer under Part I of the
      [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6) and
      any other officer or permanent employee of a prison, other than a
      penitentiary as defined in Part I of that Act;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a member of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police, a
      police officer or a police constable;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person who is designated by the Minister of
      Public Safety and Emergency Preparedness, the Commissioner of the
      Royal Canadian Mounted Police or a provincial minister as a peace
      officer for the purpose of the preservation and maintenance of the
      public peace at an aerodrome;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an officer who is enforcing any provision of the
      [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5), or of
      any regulations, warrant, order or direction made under that Act,
      respecting the arrest, detention or removal from Canada of any
      person; and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an officer or non-commissioned member of the
      Canadian Forces who is appointed as a member of the military
      police under regulations for the purposes of section 156 of the
      [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5). ([agent de la
      paix]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[personal identification code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[personal identification code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a series
    or combination of numbers, letters or other characters that is
    chosen by a person or that is assigned to a person by, or under the
    authority of, the operator of an aerodrome and that, when entered
    into or placed near mechanical or electronic equipment on or near a
    door, gate or other device, unlocks or releases the door, gate or
    other device and allows access to a restricted area. ([code
    d'identification personnel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[primary security line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[primary security line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the boundary
    between a restricted area and a non-restricted area at an
    aerodrome. ([première ligne de sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[primary security line partner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[primary security line partner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    business, organization or non-profit group --- other than the
    operator of an aerodrome, CATSA, a government department or agency
    or the police service with jurisdiction at an aerodrome --- that
    occupies an area that is on an aerodrome's primary security line and
    that includes a restricted area access point. This definition
    includes, but is not limited to, a commercial lessee of the operator
    of an aerodrome. ([partenaire de la première ligne de
    sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[regulatory requirement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[regulatory requirement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a requirement
    of

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} these Regulations;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other aviation security regulations;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a security measure;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an interim order; or

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a ministerial order. ([exigence
      réglementaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[restricted area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[restricted area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any part of an
    aerodrome that is designated as a restricted area by the operator of
    the aerodrome in accordance with a regulatory requirement. ([zone
    réglementée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[restricted area access point]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[restricted area access point]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    opening in a security barrier that allows access to a restricted
    area. ([point d'accès aux zones réglementées]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[restricted area identity card]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[restricted area identity card]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    restricted area pass that is issued by or under the authority of the
    operator of an aerodrome and that can be automatically verified by
    an identity verification system maintained by CATSA under section
    56. ([carte d'identité de zone réglementée]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[restricted area pass]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[restricted area pass]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a pass issued by
    or under the authority of the operator of an aerodrome to a person
    who requires ongoing access to restricted areas as part of the
    person's employment. ([laissez-passer de zone
    réglementée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[screening authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[screening authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, as the context
    requires,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} CATSA;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a screening contractor as defined in section 2 of
      the [Canadian Air Transport Security Authority
      Act](/eng/acts/C-11.2); or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a screening officer. ([administration de
      contrôle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[screening officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[screening officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    section 2 of the [Canadian Air Transport Security Authority
    Act](/eng/acts/C-11.2). ([agent de contrôle]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[security barrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[security barrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a physical structure
    or natural feature used to prevent or deter access by unauthorized
    persons to a restricted area. ([enceinte de sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[security personnel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[security personnel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2014-153, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

[[sensitive information respecting aviation security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sensitive information respecting aviation
    security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means information --- including
    information respecting risks, threats, vulnerabilities, systems,
    equipment, controls and procedures as well as plans and technical
    drawings --- that could be used to exploit or create a vulnerability
    or to facilitate an act or attempted act of unlawful interference
    with civil aviation. ([renseignements délicats relatifs à la sûreté
    aérienne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[specific list of prohibited items]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[specific list of prohibited items]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Part
    2 of TP 14628, which lists flights or classes of flights that
    require additional screening for reasons respecting elevated threat
    conditions or respecting the harmonization of screening rules and
    which lists or describes, for each flight or class of flight, goods
    that are supplemental to the goods listed or described in the
    general list of prohibited items. ([liste spécifique des articles
    interdits]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[sterile area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sterile area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a restricted area,
    including any passenger loading bridge attached to it, that is used
    to segregate the following persons from other persons at an
    aerodrome:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} passengers who have been screened;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} passengers who are exempted from screening in
      accordance with an aviation security regulation, a security
      measure, an emergency direction or an interim order; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} other persons who are authorized to be in the
      area by the operator of the aerodrome. ([zone
      stérile]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[threat item]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[threat item]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any of the following goods
    that are in a shipment of cargo but are not listed on the air
    waybill or other similar control document issued in respect of that
    cargo:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an explosive substance;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an incendiary device;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a component of an explosive device or incendiary
      device; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other good that could pose a threat to
      aviation security. ([article dangereux]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[TP 14628]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[TP 14628]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the document entitled
    Prohibited Items List, published by the Department of Transport in
    March 2011, as amended from time to time. ([TP
    14628]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[US inspection agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[US inspection agency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the entity
    responsible for carrying out responsibilities relating to the
    importation of goods, immigration, agriculture and public health and
    safety in order to determine whether a person or goods bound for the
    United States is or are admissible. ([organisme d'inspection des
    États-Unis]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[US inspection agency ammunition]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[US inspection agency ammunition]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    ammunition that is provided to US preclearance officers by the US
    inspection agency. ([munitions d'organisme d'inspection des
    États-Unis]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[US inspection agency firearm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[US inspection agency firearm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a firearm
    that is provided to US preclearance officers by the US inspection
    agency and that is loaded with US inspection agency ammunition or is
    unloaded. ([arme à feu d'organisme d'inspection des
    États-Unis]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[US preclearance officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[US preclearance officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
    assigned to the definition [[preclearance
    officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 5 of the [Preclearance
    Act, 2016](/eng/acts/P-19.32). ([contrôleur des
    États-Unis]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[weapon]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[weapon]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section 2
    of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46). ([arme]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 1
- SOR/2014-153, s. 2
- SOR/2015-163, s. 2
- SOR/2016-39, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 1](#1130151-1137509){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130151-1137509.html"}

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[Screening]{.HTitleText1} {#h-775408 .Part}

### [Overview]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775409 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Part overview

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out
requirements for screening officers, screening authorities and persons
who are subject to screening. This Part is also a supplement to
section 4.85 of the Act, which sets out several prohibitions respecting
screening.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1344695 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[4.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.1 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies in
respect of aerodromes listed in the schedule to the [CATSA Aerodrome
Designation Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2002-180) and in respect
of any other place designated by the Minister under subsection 6(1.1) of
the [Canadian Air Transport Security Authority Act](/eng/acts/C-11.2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 3](#1343946-1344699){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343946-1344699.html"}
:::

### [Screening Officers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775412 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  screening authority must not permit a screening officer to screen
  persons or goods unless the screening officer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is at least 18 years of age;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian citizen or a permanent resident as
    defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee
    Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is able to communicate effectively both orally and
    in writing in one or both official languages;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} has a security clearance;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} has taken the training referred to in section 20
    and, if applicable, the ongoing training referred to in section 23;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} has successfully completed the evaluation in
    accordance with section 22 and, if applicable, subsection 23(4),
    demonstrating their competence in the carrying out of their
    screening duties;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} is certified by the screening authority as meeting
    the requirements referred to in paragraphs (a) to (f); and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} is designated by the Minister under section 4.84 of
    the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supervision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must ensure that any person
  who acts or will act as a screening officer for it or on its behalf
  meets the requirements set out in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requalification --- absence of more
  than a month

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the screening officer is absent from work for more
  than one month but less than 18 months, the screening authority must
  ensure that, before the screening officer resumes their duties, the
  screening officer is evaluated and takes any necessary training on the
  training elements set out in section 20 to ensure their competency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requalification --- absence of 18
  months or more

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the screening officer is absent from work for 18
  months or more, the screening authority must ensure that the screening
  officer takes the training referred to in section 20 and is then
  evaluated to ensure their competency before they resume their duties.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 3](#1343946-1344718){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343946-1344718.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of certification

- **[[5.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  screening authority must cancel the certification granted to a person
  in accordance with paragraph 5(1)(g) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person ceases to be employed as a screening
    officer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person no longer meets the requirements set out
    in section 5 to conduct screening; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person's designation is cancelled by the
    Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must notify the Minister when
  a screening officer's certification is cancelled under paragraph
  (1)(a) or (b).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 3](#1343946-1344728){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343946-1344728.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective actions

- **[[5.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  screening authority must take corrective actions if a screening
  officer is unable to carry out their screening duties effectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The corrective actions must include a plan that
  addresses any screening deficiencies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evaluation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must evaluate the screening
  officer to ensure the screening officer's competency to carry out
  their duties effectively before they resume their duties.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 3](#1343946-1344737){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343946-1344737.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

- **[[5.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  screening authority must create a training and certification record
  for each screening officer and make it available to the Minister on
  reasonable notice given by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record keeping

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must keep the records for at
  least 90 days after the day on which the screening officer ceases to
  be employed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 3](#1343946-1344744){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343946-1344744.html"}
:::

### [Official Languages]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775423 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** At the airports that are
listed in Schedule 1 and at all aerodromes where there is a significant
demand for services in either official language within the meaning of
the [Official Languages (Communications with and Services to the Public)
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-92-48), a screening authority must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} carry out screening by means that allow effective
  communication with members of the public in the official language of
  their choice; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide in both official languages any printed or
  pre-recorded material that is used in respect of screening.

### [Carriage of Weapons, Explosive Substances and Incendiary Devices During Screening]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775428 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsection (2), a person must not present themselves for a
  screening or submit goods in their possession or control for a
  screening while they are carrying or have access to a weapon, an
  explosive substance or an incendiary device.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who is permitted under subsection 78(2) to
  carry, transport or have access to a weapon, an explosive substance or
  an incendiary device may present themselves for a screening or submit
  goods in their possession or control for a screening while they are
  carrying or have access to that weapon, substance or device.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of screening officers

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  screening authority is notified by an air carrier that a peace officer
  will be carrying or will have access to a firearm on board an
  aircraft, the screening authority must notify all the screening
  officers with whom the peace officer will come into contact that the
  peace officer will be carrying or will have access to a firearm on
  board the aircraft.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification by peace officer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the peace officer is authorized by the air carrier
  under section 531, the peace officer must present the identification
  and the form referred to in that section to the screening officers.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 8"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Identity Screening]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775441 .Subheading}

**[[8.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2022-92, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.1-20150716.html "Link to previous version of section 8.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[required
identification]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[8.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of sections 8.3 and 8.4, [[required
  identification]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one piece of valid government-issued photo
    identification that shows the holder's name and date of birth;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two pieces of valid government-issued
    identification, at least one of which shows the holder's name and
    date of birth; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a restricted area identity card.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A document issued by a government for the purposes of
  fishing, hunting or boating is not required identification.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 2
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 4](#1343948-1344754){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343948-1344754.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.2-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 8.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity screening --- age

- **[[8.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  screening authority must not allow a person to pass beyond a passenger
  screening checkpoint into a sterile area unless the screening
  authority screens the person, in accordance with these Regulations and
  any applicable security measure, emergency direction or interim order,
  by looking at the person, and in particular their entire face, to
  determine if they appear to be 18 years of age or older.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity screening --- required
  identification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A screening authority must not allow a person
  referred to in subsection (1) who appears to be 18 years of age or
  older to pass beyond a passenger screening checkpoint into a sterile
  area unless the screening authority screens the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by comparing the person, and in particular their
    entire face, against the required identification, if applicable; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by comparing the name on the person's document of
    entitlement with the name on the required identification.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 2
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 4](#1343948-1344762){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343948-1344762.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.3-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 8.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lost or stolen identification

- **[[8.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person referred to in subsection 8.3(1) who appears to be 18 years of
  age or older presents documentation that is issued by a government or
  a police service and that attests to the loss or theft of the required
  identification, a screening authority must not allow the person to
  pass beyond a passenger screening checkpoint into a sterile area
  unless the screening authority

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} screens the person's identity using alternative
    forms of identification; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} carries out an additional screening of the person,
    and of any goods in their possession or control, for goods listed or
    described in the general list of prohibited items.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examples

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Alternative forms of identification include but are
  not limited to employee identity cards, public transit passes and
  baptismal certificates.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 2
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 5](#1343950-1344764){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343950-1344764.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.4-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 8.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal of entry

- **[[8.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  screening authority must not allow a person referred to in subsection
  8.3(1) who appears to be 18 years of age or older to pass beyond a
  passenger screening checkpoint into a sterile area if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person presents a piece of photo identification
    and does not resemble the photograph;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person presents more than one form of
    identification and there is a significant discrepancy between those
    forms of identification; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is a significant discrepancy between the name
    on the identification presented by the person and the name on their
    document of entitlement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(a) does not apply in respect of a
  person referred to in subsection 8.3(1) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person's appearance changed for medical reasons
    after the photograph was taken and the person presents the screening
    authority with a document signed by a health care professional and
    attesting to that fact; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person's face is bandaged for medical reasons
    and the person presents the screening authority with a document
    signed by a health care professional and attesting to that fact.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 2
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 6](#1343952-1344770){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343952-1344770.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.5-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 8.5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Screening for Prohibited Items]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775490 .Subheading}

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92,
s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9-20150716.html "Link to previous version of section 9"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibited items

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  aviation security regulation, a security measure, an emergency
  direction or an interim order requires a person to be screened, a
  screening authority must not permit the person to pass beyond a
  screening checkpoint into a sterile area unless the screening
  authority ensures that the person is not in possession or control of
  any goods that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are listed or described in the general list of
    prohibited items; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pose an immediate threat to aviation security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specific list

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the sterile area is for passengers for a flight or
  class of flight that is listed in the specific list of prohibited
  items, the screening authority must not permit the person to pass
  beyond a screening checkpoint into the area unless the screening
  authority also ensures that the person is not in possession or control
  of any goods that are listed or described in that list for that flight
  or class of flight.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Weapons, explosive substances and
incendiary devices

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** A screening authority may
permit a person in possession or control of a weapon, an explosive
substance or an incendiary device to pass beyond a screening checkpoint
into a sterile area if the person is carrying or has access to the
weapon, explosive substance or incendiary device in accordance with
these Regulations, a security measure, an emergency direction or an
interim order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medically necessary goods

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** A screening authority may
permit a person in possession or control of goods listed or described in
the general list of prohibited items to pass beyond a screening
checkpoint into a sterile area if the goods are medically necessary and
the person declares them to the screening authority.

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92,
s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Circumventing Screening]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775508 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumventing screening

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** If an aviation security
regulation, a security measure, an emergency direction or an interim
order requires a person or any goods in their possession or control to
be screened, another person must not assist that person in circumventing
the screening.

### [Screening of Checked Baggage]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1344773 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of force

**[[14.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.1 .sectionLabel}** If a piece of checked
baggage is locked or sealed, a screening authority is authorized to use
force when it is necessary to gain access to the contents of the piece
of checked baggage for the purposes of screening.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 9](#1343959-1344792){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343959-1344792.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

- **[[14.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a screening authority uses
  force to gain access to the contents of a piece of checked baggage
  that is being screened, the screening authority must notify the person
  who tendered the checked baggage that it was opened by force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must create a record of each
  instance in which force is used to gain access to the contents of a
  piece of checked baggage that is being screened.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record keeping

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must keep the record for at
  least 180 days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of record to Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must provide the record to
  the Minister on reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 9](#1343959-1344803){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343959-1344803.html"}
:::

### [Threat Response]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775511 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Threat response

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** A screening authority at
an aerodrome who is made aware of a threat against the aerodrome must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately notify the operator of the aerodrome of
  the nature of the threat; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} assist the operator of the aerodrome in determining
  whether there is a threat that jeopardizes the security of the
  aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137809){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137809.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Reporting of Security Incidents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775516 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Items at checkpoint

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  screening authority at an aerodrome must immediately notify the
  appropriate air carrier, the operator of the aerodrome, the
  appropriate police service and the Minister if a weapon, an explosive
  substance or an incendiary device is detected at a restricted area
  access point or in any other part of the aerodrome where the screening
  of persons or goods is carried out.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a weapon,
  explosive substance or incendiary device that is permitted under
  subsection 78(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Items in checked baggage

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A screening authority at an aerodrome must
  immediately notify the appropriate air carrier, the operator of the
  aerodrome, the appropriate police service and the Minister if any of
  the following is detected in checked baggage:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a loaded firearm;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an explosive substance, other than ammunition; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an incendiary device.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incidents

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A screening authority at an aerodrome must
  immediately notify the appropriate air carrier, the operator of the
  aerodrome and the Minister of any other aviation security incident
  that involves a peace officer at a restricted area access point or in
  any other part of the aerodrome where it carries out screening.

### [Security Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775529 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security information

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** A screening authority
must provide the Minister, on reasonable notice given by the Minister,
with written or electronic records or any other information relevant to
the security of its screening operations, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} information concerning the method of implementing a
  security measure, emergency direction or interim order that applies to
  the screening authority; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the nature of the screening
  operations related to a particular flight or aerodrome.

**\[18 and 19 reserved\]**

### [Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1344805 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum training standards

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** A screening authority
must establish and implement in a consistent manner across Canada a
training program for screening officers. The training program must be
composed of both theoretical and practical training and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} cover the responsibilities and duties of a screening
  officer, as well as screening processes;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} cover the safeguarding of civil aviation operations
  against acts of unlawful interference, as well as current and emerging
  aviation security threats and trends;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} cover the use of screening equipment, and include
  practical training using the screening equipment; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} include on-the-job training provided and supervised
  by a person who has knowledge of and experience in screening.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 10](#1343961-1344813){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343961-1344813.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications of instructor

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The screening authority
must ensure that an instructor who provides the training referred to in
paragraphs 20(a) to (d)

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is at least 18 years of age;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is a Canadian citizen or a permanent resident as
  defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} has a security clearance;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} has specialized knowledge of aviation security
  screening operations;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} has successfully completed instructor training on
  aviation security and the screening elements to be taught; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} has knowledge of current and emerging aviation
  security threats and trends and of regulatory requirements related to
  screening.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 10](#1343961-1344823){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343961-1344823.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evaluation

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  screening authority must evaluate a screening officer candidate who
  has taken the training referred to in section 20 based on the
  following criteria:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} knowledge of the elements referred to in paragraphs
    20(a) and (b); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the ability to operate screening equipment and
    apply the screening processes for persons and goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evaluation --- X-ray equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the screening is to be carried out using X-ray
  equipment, the evaluation referred to in paragraph (1)(b) must contain
  an X-ray component that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the screening of goods that are listed or described
    in the general list of prohibited items, with an emphasis on
    explosive substances;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the screening of goods that are arranged in a way
    that creates various levels of difficulty in identifying them; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the use of X-ray image enhancement tools.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 10](#1343961-1344835){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343961-1344835.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ongoing training program

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  order for a screening officer to maintain their knowledge and skills,
  the screening authority must have an ongoing training program that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} covers changes in screening processes, screening
    equipment and aviation security threats and trends; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes a review of any screening issues and gaps.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frequency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The program must set out the frequency of ongoing
  training.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualified instructor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The ongoing training must be provided by an
  instructor who meets the requirements set out in section 21.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evaluation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must evaluate the knowledge
  and skills of the screening officer as soon as feasible.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 10](#1343961-1344848){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343961-1344848.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program and examinations

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** The screening authority
must provide the Minister, on reasonable notice given by the Minister,
with its screening officer training program and the examinations used
for evaluations.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 10](#1343961-1344852){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343961-1344852.html"}
:::

**\[25 to 29 reserved\]**

### [Screening Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1344854 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Screening equipment

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  screening authority must obtain the Minister's approval before
  implementing any of the following measures:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the use, on a trial basis, of new screening
    equipment or software;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the modification of approved screening equipment or
    software; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the addition of new screening equipment or
    software.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The screening authority must include the following
  information in the request for approval for the purposes of subsection
  (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for the implementation of the measures
    and any supporting documentation from the manufacturer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of any proposed modifications to
    approved screening equipment or software;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a list of the performance verification tools
    approved by the Minister that will be used; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} documentation demonstrating that the detection
    performance of any modified equipment is maintained or enhanced.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 10](#1343961-1344868){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343961-1344868.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Process --- protection of sensitive
information

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  screening authority must implement and maintain a process approved by
  the Minister for preventing the disclosure of sensitive information
  respecting aviation security when screening equipment or software is
  sold, destroyed or otherwise disposed of.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of process

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the screening authority intends to amend the
  process, it must obtain the approval of the Minister before
  implementing the amended process.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 10](#1343961-1344875){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343961-1344875.html"}
:::

**\[32 to 55 reserved\]**

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92,
s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-55-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 55"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Identity Verification System]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775539 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}System requirements

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must implement and maintain an identity verification system that is
  able to automatically verify

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that a person in possession of a restricted area
    identity card is the person to whom the card has been issued; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the restricted area identity card is active or
    has been deactivated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Biometrics

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The verification referred to in paragraph (1)(a) must
  be carried out by an on-site comparison of the biometric data provided
  by the person and a biometric template stored on the restricted area
  identity card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Database backup

**[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** CATSA must regularly back
up any database that it uses as part of the identity verification
system.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  is authorized to disclose to the Minister or the operator of an
  aerodrome any information that is necessary for the proper operation
  of the identity verification system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must not collect, use, disclose or retain the
  identity of an applicant for a restricted area identity card or the
  identity of a person to whom a restricted area identity card has been
  issued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Biometric templates

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  biometric template created from a fingerprint image or iris image
  collected from an applicant for a restricted area identity card is
  disclosed to CATSA by the operator of an aerodrome, CATSA must not use
  the biometric template for a purpose other than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} monitoring the quality of biometric templates; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determining if a restricted area identity card is
    already active in respect of the applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Already active card

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must notify the Minister if it determines that
  a restricted area identity card is already active in respect of an
  applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of templates

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must destroy any biometric template that is
  disclosed to it in connection with an application for a restricted
  area identity card as soon as feasible in accordance with the [Access
  to Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1), the [Library and Archives of
  Canada Act](/eng/acts/L-7.7), the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21) and
  any regulations made under those Acts.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** CATSA must take
appropriate measures to protect information that is collected, used,
retained or disclosed for the purposes of the identity verification
system from loss or theft and from unauthorized access, use, disclosure,
duplication or alteration.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Activation of cards

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** CATSA must activate a
restricted area identity card if the Minister informs CATSA that the
applicant for the card has a security clearance and CATSA determines
that no restricted area identity card is already active in respect of
the applicant.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deactivation of cards

**[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** CATSA must immediately
deactivate a restricted area identity card if the Minister or the
operator of an aerodrome asks CATSA to deactivate the card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Business continuity plan

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must develop and maintain a business continuity plan --- to be
  implemented in the event that it is unable to use the identity
  verification system --- that sets out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} how CATSA will maintain an equivalent level of
    security until normal operations are re-established, including the
    ability to meet the following objectives:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to receive security clearance information from
      the Minister,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to activate and deactivate restricted area
      identity cards, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to allow the operator of an aerodrome to verify
      that a restricted area identity card is active or has been
      deactivated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} how CATSA will re-establish normal operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must implement its business continuity plan and
  immediately notify the Minister and any affected operator of an
  aerodrome if it discovers that it is unable to use the identity
  verification system for the operations set out in subparagraphs
  (1)(a)(i) to (iii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delay notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must immediately notify the Minister and any
  affected operator of an aerodrome if it discovers that it will be
  unable, for more than 24 hours, to use the identity verification
  system for the operations set out in subparagraphs (1)(a)(i) to (iii)
  and must immediately inform the Minister and any affected operator of
  an aerodrome of how it will re-establish normal operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must make its business continuity plan
  available to the Minister on reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 12](#1343964-1344891){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343964-1344891.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-63-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 63"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must keep updated records respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    activated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    deactivated;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} deactivated restricted area identity cards that
    have not been retrieved;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    reported as lost or stolen; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} blank restricted area identity cards distributed to
    operators of aerodromes.

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of records to Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must provide the Minister with the records on
  reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 13](#1343966-1344896){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343966-1344896.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-64-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 64"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[CATSA Security Program]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1388630 .Part}

### [CATSA Security Program Requirements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1388631 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to establish and implement

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must establish and implement a security program in order to safeguard
  information, equipment, information technology infrastructure,
  facilities and all other assets related to screening operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As part of its security program, CATSA must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} develop a security policy statement that
    establishes an overall security commitment and direction and sets
    out the security objectives;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a security awareness
    program that promotes a culture of vigilance among its employees;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} conduct a security risk assessment in accordance
    with section 69;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} assess risk information and disseminate it within
    its organization for the purpose of facilitating informed
    decision-making about security;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} establish a strategic security plan in accordance
    with section 73;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} identify the security official referred to in
    subsection (3);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} subject to section 31, establish and implement a
    process for receiving, identifying, retaining, disclosing and
    disposing of sensitive information respecting aviation security in
    order to protect the information from unauthorized access; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a process for responding to
    security incidents and breaches.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security official

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must have, at all times, at least one security
  official or acting security official who is responsible for acting as
  the principal contact between CATSA and the Minister with respect to
  the security program.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388648){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388648.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must keep

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} documentation related to its security risk
    assessment and any review of it for at least five years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} documentation related to its strategic security
    plan and any amendment to it for at least five years; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all other documentation related to its security
    program for at least two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must make the documentation available to the
  Minister on reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388658){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388658.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to amend

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** CATSA must amend its
security program if it identifies a security risk that is not addressed
by the program.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388662){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388662.html"}
:::

### [Committees]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1388663 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multi-agency advisory committee

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** CATSA must be an active
member of an aerodrome's multi-agency advisory committee referred to in
sections 196 and 353.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388671){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388671.html"}
:::

### [Security Risk Assessments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1388672 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security risk assessments

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** CATSA must have a
security risk assessment that identifies and assesses the risks in
respect of information, equipment, information technology
infrastructure, facilities and all other assets related to screening
operations and that includes the following elements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a threat assessment that evaluates the probability of
  a disruption to security screening services caused by an unauthorized
  access or an act or attempted act of unlawful interference;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a criticality assessment that prioritizes the
  information, equipment, information technology infrastructure,
  facilities and all other assets related to screening operations that
  most require protection from acts and attempted acts of unlawful
  interference;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a vulnerability assessment that considers the extent
  to which information, equipment, information technology
  infrastructure, facilities and all other assets related to screening
  operations are susceptible to loss or damage; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an impact assessment that, at a minimum, measures the
  consequences of a security screening incident or potential security
  screening incident in terms of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a decrease in public safety and security,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} financial and economic loss, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a loss of public confidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388683){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388683.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission for approval

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must submit its initial security risk assessment to the Minister for
  approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission --- five years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must submit a new security risk assessment to
  the Minister within five years after the date of the most recent
  approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388690){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388690.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security risk assessment --- annual
review

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must conduct a review of its security risk assessment at least once a
  year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security risk assessment --- other
  reviews

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must also conduct a review of its security risk
  assessment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it acquires new equipment, information technology
    infrastructure, facilities or any other new assets related to
    screening operations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it makes changes to security screening operations
    that could affect information, equipment, information technology
    infrastructure, facilities or any other assets related to screening
    operations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a change in regulatory requirements could affect
    information, equipment, information technology infrastructure,
    facilities or any other assets related to screening operations; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it identifies a vulnerability that is not addressed
    in the assessment or the Minister identifies such a vulnerability to
    CATSA.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equivalency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a review conducted under
  subsection (2) counts as a review required under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When CATSA conducts a review of its security risk
  assessment, it must document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any decision to amend or to not amend the
    assessment or the risk-management strategy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for that decision; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the factors that were taken into consideration in
    making that decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must notify the Minister if, as a result of a
  review of its security risk assessment, it amends the assessment

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to include a new medium to high risk; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to raise or lower the level of a risk within the
    medium to high range.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388712){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388712.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must approve
a security risk assessment submitted by CATSA if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assessment meets the requirements of section 69;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the assessment has been reviewed by an executive
  within CATSA who is responsible for security;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} CATSA has considered all available and relevant
  information; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} CATSA has not overlooked a security risk that could
  compromise information, equipment, information technology
  infrastructure, facilities or any other assets related to screening
  operations.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388720){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388720.html"}
:::

### [Strategic Security Plans]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1388721 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Strategic security plans

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  must establish a strategic security plan that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} summarizes its strategy to prepare for, detect,
    prevent, respond to and recover from threats identified in the
    security risk assessment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes a risk-management strategy that addresses
    the medium to high security risks identified and prioritized in its
    security risk assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission for approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must submit its strategic security plan to the
  Minister for approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of plan

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must approve a strategic security plan
  submitted by CATSA if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plan meets the requirements of subsection (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the plan has been reviewed by an executive within
    CATSA who is responsible for security;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the plan is likely to enable CATSA to prepare for,
    detect, prevent, respond to and recover from an unauthorized access
    to or acts or attempted acts of unlawful interference with
    information, equipment, information technology infrastructure,
    facilities or any other assets related to screening operations;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the risk-management strategy is in proportion to
    the risks it addresses;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} CATSA has not overlooked a security risk that could
    compromise information, equipment, information technology
    infrastructure, facilities or any other assets related to screening
    operations; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the plan can be implemented without compromising
    security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} CATSA must, as soon as its strategic security plan is
  approved, implement its risk-management strategy.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388740){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388740.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} CATSA
  may amend its strategic security plan at any time, but must do so if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plan does not reflect CATSA's most recent
    security risk assessment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs CATSA that there is a change
    in the threat environment that could result in a new or unaddressed
    medium to high risk to information, equipment, information
    technology infrastructure, facilities or any other assets related to
    screening operations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} CATSA identifies a deficiency in the plan; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs CATSA that its risk-management
    strategy is not in proportion to a risk set out in its security risk
    assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If CATSA amends its strategic security plan, it must
  document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reason for the amendment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the factors that were taken into consideration in
    making that amendment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of amendment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If CATSA amends its strategic security plan, it must,
  as soon as possible, submit the amendment to the Minister for
  approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must approve an amendment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an amendment to the summary required
    under paragraph 73(1)(a), the conditions set out in paragraphs
    73(3)(a) to (c) have been met; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an amendment to the risk-management
    strategy required under paragraph 73(1)(b), the conditions set out
    in paragraphs 73(3)(a) to (f) have been met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If CATSA amends its risk-management strategy, it must
  implement the amended version of the strategy once it is approved by
  the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388761){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388761.html"}
:::

### [Disclosure of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1388762 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** A person other than the
Minister must not disclose security-sensitive information that is
created or used under this Part unless the disclosure is required by law
or is necessary to comply or facilitate compliance with the aviation
security provisions of the Act, regulatory requirements or the
requirements of an emergency direction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 14](#1343967-1388770){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343967-1388770.html"}
:::

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Weapons, Explosive Substances and Incendiary Devices]{.HTitleText1} {#h-775593 .Part}

### [Overview]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775594 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Part overview

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out
prohibitions respecting weapons, explosive substances and incendiary
devices at aerodromes and on board aircraft. This Part also sets out
exceptions to those prohibitions.

### [At Aerodromes]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775597 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- sale

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** A person must not sell or
offer for sale any of the following goods in a restricted area:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a weapon;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a model or replica of a weapon;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an explosive substance; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an incendiary device.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- carriage, transportation
and access

- **[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  must not carry, transport or have access to a weapon, an explosive
  substance or an incendiary device at an aerodrome.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person described in column 1 of the table to this
  subsection may, at an aerodrome, carry, transport or have access to
  the goods set out in column 2 if the conditions set out in column 3
  are met.

  <figure id="775609">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize7 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
  scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom" scope="col">Column
  1</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom" scope="col">Column
  2</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom bottom" scope="col">Column 3</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Person</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Permitted
  Goods</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Conditions</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="row">any person</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">an unloaded firearm</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">the person is carrying or transporting
  the unloaded firearm, or has access to it, for the purpose of
  transporting it by air as checked baggage or cargo</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>

  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">a weapon and

  <td class="noBorderBottom top">the peace officer is acting in the course

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consumption of alcoholic beverages

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employee of a federal or provincial department or
  agency who is engaged in wildlife control, and who carries, transports
  or has access to an unloaded firearm at an aerodrome, must not consume
  any alcoholic beverage.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[commercial
  office]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), [[commercial
  office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area of a customs office
  designated under section 5 of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) that
  is used primarily for the reporting, examination and release under
  that Act of [[commercial goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
  section 2 of the [Reporting of Imported Goods
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-873).
