[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oversight and corrective actions

- **[[78.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The agency that employs the person
  described in items 10 to 21, column 1, of the table to subsection
  78(2) must ensure that that person meets the conditions set out in
  column 3 of those items and, in the case where the agency identifies a
  failure to comply with a condition, it must immediately take
  corrective actions to prevent the recurrence of the failure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records --- failure to comply with
  condition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each time the agency identifies a failure to comply
  with a condition, it must create a record that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the failure occurred;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the failure; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the corrective actions that were
    taken to prevent the recurrence of the failure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of records to Minister

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The agency must provide the Minister with the records
  on reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-268, s. 3](#1381976-1387543){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1381976-1387543.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records --- exigent circumstances

- **[[78.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each time a person described in item
  12 or 19, column 1, of the table to subsection 78(2) exceeds, due to
  exigent circumstances, the one-hour period referred to in column 3,
  the agency that employs that person must create a record that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the one-hour period was exceeded;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount of time by which the one-hour period was
    exceeded; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the exigent circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of records to Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The agency must provide the Minister with the records
  on reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-268, s. 3](#1381976-1387553){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1381976-1387553.html"}
:::

### [On Board Aircraft]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775617 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Weapons

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2.1) to (4),
  a person must not carry or have access to a weapon on board an
  aircraft.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Explosive substances and incendiary
  devices

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2.1) to (2.4), (3.1) and (4),
  a person other than an air carrier must not carry or have access to an
  explosive substance or incendiary device on board an aircraft.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- air carrier flights

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A Canadian in-flight security officer who is acting
  in the course of their duties may carry or have access to a loaded
  firearm on board an aircraft operated by an air carrier.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- border services officer

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} A border services officer who is acting in the
  course of their duties may carry or have access to defensive
  equipment, an agency firearm and ammunition on board an aircraft that
  is on the ground at an aerodrome.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- US preclearance officer

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} A US preclearance officer who is exercising their
  powers or performing their duties or functions under the [Preclearance
  Act, 2016](/eng/acts/P-19.32) may carry or have access to defensive
  equipment, a US inspection agency firearm and US inspection agency
  ammunition on board an aircraft that is on the ground at an aerodrome.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- certain federal employees

  [(2.4)]{.lawlabel} A person described in item 22, column 1, of the
  table to subsection 78(2) who is acting in the course of their duties
  may carry or have access to defensive equipment, a loaded agency
  firearm and ammunition on board an aircraft that is on the ground at
  an aerodrome.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- air carrier flights

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following persons may carry or have access to an
  unloaded firearm on board an aircraft operated by an air carrier:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a peace officer who is authorized by the air
    carrier under section 531;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the pilot-in-command of the aircraft if authorized
    by the air carrier under subsection 533(1); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an employee of a federal or provincial department
    or agency who is engaged in wildlife control and who is authorized
    by the air carrier under subsection 533(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- certain federal employees

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A person described in item 22, column 1, of the
  table to subsection 78(2) may carry or have access to defensive
  equipment, a loaded agency firearm and ammunition on board an
  aircraft, if authorized by the operator of the aircraft under section
  533.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- other operators' flights

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The pilot-in-command of an aircraft operated by a
  person other than an air carrier may carry or have access to an
  unloaded firearm and ammunition on board the aircraft if authorized by
  the operator of the aircraft under subsection 533(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consumption of alcoholic beverages

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The following persons must not consume any alcoholic
  beverage if they are on board an aircraft and are carrying or have
  access to an unloaded firearm:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a peace officer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an employee of a federal or provincial department
    or agency who is engaged in wildlife control.

### [Transport and Tendering for Transportation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775638 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General prohibitions

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a person
  must not transport any of the following goods on board an aircraft
  operated by an air carrier or tender them for transportation by an air
  carrier:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a loaded firearm;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an explosive substance other than ammunition; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an incendiary device.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unloaded firearms

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not tender an unloaded firearm to an
  air carrier for subsequent acceptance and transportation unless the
  person declares to the air carrier that the firearm is unloaded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person may transport an explosive substance or an
  incendiary device on board an aircraft operated by an air carrier, or
  tender it for transportation by an air carrier, if the person notifies
  the air carrier before the explosive substance or incendiary device
  arrives at the aerodrome.

### [False Declarations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775649 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False declarations

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** A person at an aerodrome
or on board an aircraft must not falsely declare

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the person is carrying a weapon, an explosive
  substance, an incendiary device or any other item that could be used
  to jeopardize the security of an aerodrome or aircraft or that such an
  item is contained in goods in the person's possession or control or in
  goods that the person has tendered or is tendering for screening or
  transportation; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} that another person at an aerodrome or on board an
  aircraft is carrying a weapon, an explosive substance, an incendiary
  device or any other item that could be used to jeopardize the security
  of an aerodrome or aircraft or that such an item is contained in goods
  in the other person's possession or control or in goods that the other
  person has tendered or is tendering for screening or transportation.

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Class 1 Aerodromes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-775654 .Part}

### [Overview]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775655 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Part overview

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out the
basic regulatory framework for security at aerodromes listed in
Schedule 1.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775658 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies in
respect of aerodromes listed in Schedule 1.

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Prohibited Items]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775661 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775662 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** This Division completes
and supplements the regulatory framework set out in Part 3.

#### [Authorization for Carriage of or Access to Explosive Substances and Incendiary Devices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775665 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome may authorize a person to carry or have access to an explosive
substance or an incendiary device at the aerodrome if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosive substance or incendiary device is to be
  used at the aerodrome

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for excavation, demolition or construction work,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in fireworks displays,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} by persons operating explosives detection
    equipment or handling explosives detection dogs,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} by a police service, or

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} by military personnel; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator has reasonable grounds to believe that
  the safety of the aerodrome and the safety of persons and aircraft at
  the aerodrome will not be jeopardized by the presence of the explosive
  substance or incendiary device.

#### [Availability of Prohibited Items]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775675 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- sterile area

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must not permit goods listed or described in
  the general list of prohibited items or, as applicable, the specific
  list of prohibited items to be made available to persons in a sterile
  area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- liquids, aerosols and
  gels

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of liquids,
  aerosols and gels that are made available to persons in accordance
  with a security measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- knives

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of rounded,
  dull-blade knives and plastic knives that are made available to the
  customers of a concessionaire with the permission of the operator of
  the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 3
:::

**\[87 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Threats and Incidents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775686 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775687 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the regulatory framework for dealing with threats and incidents at an
aerodrome.

#### [Threat Response]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775690 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Area under operator's control

**[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who is made aware of a threat against an aviation facility, or
a part of the aerodrome, that is under the operator's control must
immediately determine whether there is a threat that jeopardizes the
security of the facility or that part of the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137813){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137813.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-89-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 89"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Area under control of other person

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who is made aware of a threat against an aviation facility, or
a part of the aerodrome, that is under the control of a person carrying
on any activity at the aerodrome, other than the operator, must
immediately

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} notify the person of the nature of the threat; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} determine whether there is a threat that jeopardizes
  the security of the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137816){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137816.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-90-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 90"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Threats

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who determines that there is a threat that jeopardizes the
security of the aerodrome must immediately take all of the measures
necessary to ensure the safety of the aerodrome and persons at the
aerodrome, including informing the appropriate police service of the
nature of the threat.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137820){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137820.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-91-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 91"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of other person

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** A person, other than a
screening authority, who is carrying on any activity at an aerodrome and
who is made aware of a threat against the aerodrome must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately notify the operator of the aerodrome of
  the nature of the threat; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} assist the operator of the aerodrome in determining
  whether there is a threat that jeopardizes the security of the
  aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137823){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137823.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-92-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 92"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Threats identified by other person

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** If it is determined under
paragraph 15(b), 90(b) or 92(b) that there is a threat that jeopardizes
the security of an aerodrome, the operator of the aerodrome must
immediately take all of the measures necessary to ensure the safety of
the aerodrome and persons at the aerodrome, including informing the
appropriate police service of the nature of the threat.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137827){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137827.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-93-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 93"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Information Reporting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775705 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security incidents

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must immediately notify the Minister when any of the following
incidents occur:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the discovery, at the aerodrome, of a weapon,
  explosive substance or incendiary device that is not permitted under
  subsection 78(2);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an explosion at the aerodrome, unless the explosion
  is known to be the result of an accident, excavation, demolition or
  construction work, or the use of fireworks displays;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a threat against the aerodrome; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an aviation security incident that involves a peace
  officer anywhere at the aerodrome other than areas under an air
  carrier's control.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137830){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137830.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-94-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commercial air service information

**[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must provide the Minister with written notice of any new
commercial air service that is to begin at an air terminal building.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[AVSEC Levels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775714 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775715 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting the implementation of additional safeguards in
the event of heightened risk conditions.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 5
:::

#### [AVSEC Level Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775720 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional safeguards

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** If the AVSEC level is
raised or maintained above level 1 for an aerodrome or any part of an
aerodrome, the operator of the aerodrome must immediately take the
following actions:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine which additional safeguards are likely to
  mitigate the heightened risk condition;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify any persons or organizations that have
  aviation security roles and responsibilities at the aerodrome and are
  affected by the heightened risk condition;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} implement or continue to implement the additional
  safeguards; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} notify the Minister of the additional safeguards that
  are being or will be implemented.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** When the AVSEC level is
lowered for an aerodrome or any part of an aerodrome, the operator of
the aerodrome must immediately notify the persons and organizations that
were notified under paragraph 97(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Legal powers and obligations

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
nothing in these Regulations authorizes the operator of an aerodrome to
implement additional safeguards that are inconsistent with the
operator's legal powers and obligations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 5
:::

**\[100 to 107 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Personnel and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775738 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775739 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting aerodrome security personnel and other persons
who are assigned aerodrome-related security roles and responsibilities
at an aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 75
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-108-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 108"){.PITLink}
:::

**\[109 and 110 reserved\]**

#### [Security Official]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775745 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-153, s. 6

\]
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** A security official of
an aerodrome is an individual who is responsible for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} coordinating and overseeing security controls and
  procedures at the aerodrome; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} acting as the principal contact between the operator
  of the aerodrome and the Minister with respect to security matters,
  including the airport security program.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must have, at all times, at least one
  security official or acting security official.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contact information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must provide the
  Minister with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of each security official and acting
    security official; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 24-hour contact information for those officials.

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2014-153, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-113-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 113"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2014-153, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-114-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 114"){.PITLink}
:::

**\[113 and 114 reserved\]**

#### [Aerodrome Security Personnel]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775762 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial training

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that a member of the aerodrome
  security personnel does not carry out an aerodrome-related security
  role or responsibility at the aerodrome unless the member has received
  initial training in relation to that role or responsibility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training elements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Initial training for aerodrome security personnel
  must include instruction and evaluation in relation to the topics set
  out below that are relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles
  and responsibilities of the personnel:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} international instruments respecting aviation
    security, the aviation security provisions of the Act and regulatory
    requirements;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the security controls and procedures at the
    aerodrome where the personnel are employed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} systems and equipment at the aerodrome;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an overview of threats to aviation security and
    acts or attempted acts of unlawful interference with civil aviation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of goods that are listed or
    described in TP 14628 or that pose an immediate threat to aviation
    security; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the actions to be taken by the personnel in
    response to a threat to aviation security or an act or attempted act
    of unlawful interference with civil aviation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grandfathering

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Aerodrome security personnel who are employed at the
  aerodrome on the day on which this section comes into force are
  exempted from initial training in relation to any topic for which they
  have already received training.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Follow-up training

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that aerodrome security personnel
  receive follow-up training when any of the following circumstances
  arise:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a change is made in the aviation security
    provisions of the Act or in regulatory requirements and the change
    is relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles and
    responsibilities of the personnel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a change is made in the security controls and
    procedures at the aerodrome where the personnel are employed and the
    change is relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles and
    responsibilities of the personnel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a new or modified action is to be taken by the
    personnel in response to a threat to aviation security or an act or
    attempted act of unlawful interference with civil aviation; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a significant risk or an emerging trend in aviation
    security is identified to the operator by the Minister and the risk
    or trend is relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles and
    responsibilities of the personnel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Follow-up training

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must ensure that a
  member of the aerodrome security personnel receives follow-up training
  when the Minister or the operator identifies a shortcoming in the
  member's performance when the member is carrying out security controls
  or following security procedures at the aerodrome.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training elements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Follow-up training must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a review of any initial-training element related to
    the circumstance set out in subsection (1) or (2) that gave rise to
    the follow-up training; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} instruction and evaluation in relation to that
    circumstance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}On-the-job training

**[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** If, at an aerodrome,
the initial or follow-up training of aerodrome security personnel
includes on-the-job training, the operator of the aerodrome must ensure
that the on-the-job training is provided by a person who has received
that same training or has significant experience working as a member of
the aerodrome security personnel at an aerodrome listed in Schedule 1.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training records

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that, for each individual who
  receives training in accordance with section 115 or 116, there is a
  training record that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the individual's employee group or contractor
    group, if applicable, and a description of the individual's
    aerodrome-related security roles and responsibilities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of all the training that the
    individual has received in accordance with section 115 or 116; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} evaluation results for all the training that the
    individual has received in accordance with section 115 or 116.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record keeping

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must keep the training
  record for at least two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must make the training
  record available to the Minister on reasonable notice given by the
  Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 8
:::

**\[119 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Facilitation of Screening]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775810 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775811 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting the facilitation of screening operations at an
aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 4
:::

#### [Screening of Passengers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775816 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passenger screening facilities

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for passenger screening
checkpoints and must make at least one facility available for the
private screening of passengers.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 4
- SOR/2014-153, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-121-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 121"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False declaration notice

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must post a notice at each passenger
  screening checkpoint stating that it is an offence for a person at the
  aerodrome to falsely declare

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the person is carrying a weapon, an explosive
    substance, an incendiary device or any other item that could be used
    to jeopardize the security of an aerodrome or aircraft, or that such
    an item is contained in goods in the person's possession or control
    or in goods that the person has tendered or is tendering for
    screening or transportation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that another person who is at an aerodrome or on
    board an aircraft is carrying a weapon, an explosive substance, an
    incendiary device or any other item that could be used to jeopardize
    the security of an aerodrome or aircraft, or that such an item is
    contained in goods in the other person's possession or control or in
    goods that the other person has tendered or is tendering for
    screening or transportation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice must be clearly visible and be in at least
  both official languages.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 4
- SOR/2014-153, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Screening of Non-passengers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775830 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-passenger screening facilities

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for non-passenger screening
checkpoints at restricted area access points and at locations inside
restricted areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-passenger access to restricted areas

**[[123.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.1 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must, in accordance with a security measure, ensure that
non-passengers enter a restricted area at the aerodrome only through a
restricted area access point where screening is carried out.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-161, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- liquids, aerosols or gels

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that non-passengers who access sterile areas are
notified of any restrictions on the possession of liquids, aerosols or
gels in sterile areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 4
:::

#### [Screening of Checked Baggage]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775843 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Checked baggage screening facilities

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for the screening of checked
baggage and baggage intended to be checked baggage.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 4
- SOR/2014-153, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-125-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 125"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Baggage Handling Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775848 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No change without agreement

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** If the operator of an
aerodrome is responsible for a baggage handling system, the operator
must not make any change to the system that may affect screening
operations unless the change is agreed to by CATSA.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 4
:::

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Access Controls]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775853 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775854 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the regulatory framework for the protection of security-sensitive areas
of aerodromes.

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775857 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sign requirements

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must post signs on the outside of each
  restricted area access point and each security barrier. Each sign must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in at least both official languages;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the restricted area as a restricted area;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} state that access to the area is restricted to
    authorized persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signs on security barriers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The signs posted on a security barrier must be no
  more than 150 m apart.

#### [Restricted Area Access Points]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775866 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access control system

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that each restricted area access point that allows
access from a non-restricted area to a restricted area has an access
control system consisting of one or more of the following elements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} surveillance by a person authorized by the operator
  of the aerodrome to control access to the restricted area;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} manual locking equipment; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} automated access control equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passenger loading bridge

**[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that each restricted area access point that is
located between an air terminal building and a passenger loading bridge
has a door that can be locked.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** A person must not enter
a restricted area at an aerodrome except through a restricted area
access point.

#### [Baggage Handling Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775880 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prevention of unauthorized access

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must take measures to prevent unauthorized access to a baggage
handling system that is in a restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 6
:::

#### [Doors, Gates, Emergency Exits and Other Devices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775885 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to close and lock --- operators

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must close and lock any door, gate or other
  device, other than an emergency exit, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator has control of and responsibility for
    the door, gate or other device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door, gate or other device allows access
    between a restricted area and a non-restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exit system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must institute a system,
  on or near an emergency exit, that prevents access by unauthorized
  persons to a restricted area, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator has control of and responsibility for
    the emergency exit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emergency exit allows access between a
    restricted area and a non-restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to close and lock --- partners and
lessees

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  primary security line partner, or a lessee other than a primary
  security line partner, at an aerodrome must close and lock any door,
  gate or other device, other than an emergency exit, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the partner or lessee has control of and
    responsibility for the door, gate or other device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door, gate or other device allows access
    between a restricted area and a non-restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exit system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A primary security line partner who occupies an area
  on an aerodrome's primary security line must institute a system, on or
  near an emergency exit, that prevents access by unauthorized persons
  to a restricted area, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the partner has control of and responsibility for
    the emergency exit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emergency exit allows access between a
    restricted area and a non-restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary use or control

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** Any person at an
aerodrome who has temporary use or control of a door, gate or other
device that allows access between a restricted area and a non-restricted
area must prevent access to or from the restricted area by unauthorized
persons.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Uncontrolled restricted area access point

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** Unless an authorized
person is controlling access between a restricted area and a
non-restricted area at an aerodrome, a person who enters or leaves the
restricted area must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} lock the door, gate or other device that allows
  access to or from the restricted area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent access to or from the restricted area by
  unauthorized persons while the door, gate or other device is open or
  unlocked.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preventing locking

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** A person at an
aerodrome must not prevent a door, gate or other device, other than an
emergency exit, that allows access between a restricted area and a
non-restricted area from being locked.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exits

**[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** A person at an
aerodrome must not open any door that is designated as an emergency exit
and that is also a restricted area access point unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is authorized by the operator of the
  aerodrome to open it; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is an emergency.

#### [Unauthorized Access]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775916 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person has been given notice, orally, in writing or by a sign, that
  access to a part of an aerodrome is prohibited or is limited to
  authorized persons, the person must not enter or remain in that part
  of the aerodrome without authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restricted areas

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may authorize a person
  to enter or remain in a restricted area if the requirements of
  Divisions 6 to 8 are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public areas other than restricted
  areas

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may authorize a person
  to enter or remain in a part of the aerodrome that is not a public
  area but is not a restricted area if the safety of the aerodrome,
  persons at the aerodrome and aircraft is not jeopardized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public areas other than restricted
  areas

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A lessee at an aerodrome who has the use of, or is
  responsible for, a part of the aerodrome that is not a public area but
  is not a restricted area may authorize a person to enter or remain in
  that part of the aerodrome if the safety of the aerodrome, persons at
  the aerodrome and aircraft is not jeopardized.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-139-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 139"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Inspectors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775928 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to allow access

**[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must allow an inspector to enter or remain in a restricted
area if the inspector is acting in the course of their employment and
presents their credentials.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 7
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Documents of Entitlement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775933 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
provisions respecting documents of entitlement. Requirements respecting
the issue and use of restricted area identity cards are set out in
Division 8.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of documents

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Only
  the following documents are documents of entitlement at an aerodrome:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a restricted area identity card;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a temporary pass issued by the operator of the
    aerodrome;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a boarding pass, a ticket, or any other document
    accepted by an air carrier that confirms the status of the person to
    whom it was issued as a passenger on a flight and that is approved
    by the operator of the aerodrome;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a passenger escort form that is approved by the
    operator of the aerodrome;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a courtesy-lounge or conference-room pass that is
    issued by an air carrier and that is approved by the operator of the
    aerodrome; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a document that is issued or approved by the
    operator of the aerodrome in accordance with a security measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pilot's licence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A pilot's licence issued under the [Canadian Aviation
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-96-433) is a document of entitlement
  for a restricted area that is used by general aviation, if the holder
  of the licence also holds a valid medical certificate of a category
  that is appropriate for that licence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is acting in the course of their employment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requires access to an aircraft that they own or
    operate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 8
- SOR/2014-153, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Enhanced Access Controls]{.HTitleText2} {#h-775953 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775954 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
enhanced access control requirements, including requirements respecting
the identity verification system referred to in section 56.

#### [Identity Verification System]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775957 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome is authorized to disclose to the Minister or
  CATSA any information that is necessary for the proper operation of
  the identity verification system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the operator of an aerodrome
  must not disclose to CATSA the identity of an applicant for a
  restricted area identity card or the identity of a person to whom a
  restricted area identity card has been issued unless the operator
  grants CATSA access to its databases to maintain or repair the
  identity verification system and CATSA's access to the person's
  identity is incidental to the maintenance or repairs.

#### [Information To Be Displayed on a Restricted Area Identity Card]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775963 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required information

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that the following information is
  displayed on each restricted area identity card that it issues:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the full name of the person to whom the card is
    issued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 15\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a photograph depicting a frontal view of the
    person's face;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the expiry date of the card;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the aerodrome where the card is issued;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person's employer, if the person
    has a single employer;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the terms "multi-employer" and "employeur
    multiple", if the person has more than one employer.

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 15\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 15\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiration date

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A restricted area identity card, including one that
  is issued to a person who requires access to restricted areas at more
  than one aerodrome, expires no later than five years after the day on
  which it is issued or on the day on which the security clearance of
  the person to whom the card is issued expires, whichever is earlier.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 15\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must ensure that all
  information that is displayed on a restricted area identity card is in
  both official languages.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 15](#1343969-1344905){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343969-1344905.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-145-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 145"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Issuance of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-775982 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance criteria

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must not issue a restricted area identity
  card to a person unless the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applies in writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is sponsored in writing by their employer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has a security clearance;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} consents in writing to the collection, use,
    retention, disclosure and destruction of information for the
    purposes of this Division; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} confirms that the information displayed on the card
    is correct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Activation requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must not issue a
  restricted area identity card to a person unless the card has been
  activated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False information

**[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** A person must not
provide false information for the purpose of obtaining a restricted area
identity card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sponsorship

**[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** An employer must not

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} sponsor an employee who does not require ongoing
  access to restricted areas in the course of their employment; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} knowingly sponsor an employee for more than one
  restricted area identity card at a time.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of multiple cards

**[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must not issue more than one restricted area identity card at
a time to a person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement of cards

**[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** Before replacing a
lost, stolen or non-functional restricted area identity card, the
operator of an aerodrome must ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person applying for the replacement card is the
  person to whom the lost, stolen or non-functional card has been
  issued; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person still has a security clearance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to inform

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** Before collecting
information from an applicant under this Division, the operator of an
aerodrome must bring to the applicant's attention the purposes for which
the information is collected and the manner in which the information
will be used, retained, disclosed and destroyed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection of information

- **[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of creating a restricted area identity card for an
  applicant, the operator of an aerodrome must collect the following
  information from the applicant:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's full name;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's height;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a photograph depicting a frontal view of the
    applicant's face;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's fingerprint images and iris images;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the applicant's employer; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's occupation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of images and templates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must, immediately after
  issuing the restricted area identity card, destroy all fingerprint
  images and iris images that the operator collected from the applicant
  and any biometric template created from those images that is not
  stored on the card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quality control

**[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
allowing CATSA to monitor the quality of biometric templates and
determining if a restricted area identity card is already active in
respect of an applicant, the operator of an aerodrome must, before
issuing the card, disclose to CATSA any biometric templates created from
the fingerprint images and iris images collected from the applicant.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must take appropriate measures to protect information that is
collected, used, retained or disclosed in accordance with this Division
from loss or theft and from unauthorized access, use, disclosure,
duplication or alteration.

#### [Deactivation of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776022 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deactivation request

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome who has issued a restricted area identity
  card must immediately ask CATSA to deactivate the card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card expires;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom the card has been issued or
    their employer informs the operator that the card is lost, stolen or
    no longer functional; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom the card has been issued fails,
    on demand, to present or surrender the card to a screening officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reason for deactivation

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of an aerodrome asks CATSA to
  deactivate a restricted area identity card, the operator must inform
  CATSA of the reason for the request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must not ask CATSA to
  deactivate a restricted area identity card for a reason other than a
  reason set out in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of Minister

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must notify the Minister
  if the operator asks CATSA to deactivate a restricted area identity
  card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-155-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 155"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in employment

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who has issued a restricted area identity card must notify the
Minister immediately if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has a single employer,
  the person to whom the card has been issued ceases to be employed or
  no longer requires ongoing access to restricted areas in the course of
  their employment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has more than one
  employer, the person to whom the card has been issued ceases to be
  employed by all of their employers or no longer requires ongoing
  access to restricted areas in the course of their employment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of employer

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** The employer of a
person to whom a restricted area identity card has been issued must
immediately notify the operator of an aerodrome who issued the card if
the person ceases to be an employee or no longer requires ongoing access
to restricted areas in the course of their employment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retrieval of cards

- **[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome who has issued a restricted area identity
  card must take reasonable steps to retrieve the card if it has been
  deactivated and must notify CATSA if the card is not retrieved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of cards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a restricted area identity card has been
  deactivated, the person to whom the card has been issued must
  immediately return it to the operator of an aerodrome who issued it
  unless the card was surrendered in accordance with this Division or
  was lost or stolen.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of cards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must, as soon as
  feasible, destroy a restricted area identity card that has been
  retrieved or returned.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 16](#1343975-1344909){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343975-1344909.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-158-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 158"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Keys, Combination Codes and Personal Identification Codes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776048 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance or assignment

**[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must not issue a key or assign a combination code or personal
identification code to a person for a restricted area unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is a person to whom a restricted area
  identity card has been issued and the card is active; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person is in possession of a document that is
  issued or approved by the operator of the aerodrome in accordance with
  a security measure as authorization for the person to enter or remain
  in the restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Addition of key

**[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome may add a key to a restricted area identity card only if it is
possible to cancel or remove the key without damaging or altering any
other elements of the card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information

**[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must not add to or modify a restricted area identity card in
any way that might allow the disclosure to CATSA of information about
the person to whom the card has been issued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation, withdrawal or retrieval

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must cancel, withdraw or retrieve a key that has been issued
to a person who has been issued a restricted area identity card, or a
combination code or personal identification code that has been assigned
to that person, if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person's restricted area identity card has been
  deactivated; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person no longer requires ongoing access to the
  restricted area in the course of their employment.

#### [Records]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776061 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General requirement

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome and any person designated by the operator to
  issue restricted area identity cards or keys or to assign combination
  codes or personal identification codes must keep updated records at
  the aerodrome respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards and keys that have
    been issued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the names of the persons to whom restricted area
    identity cards or keys have been issued;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the names of the persons to whom combination codes
    or personal identification codes have been assigned;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} blank restricted area identity cards in the
    operator's possession;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    deactivated;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} keys, combination codes or personal identification
    codes that have been cancelled, withdrawn or retrieved;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} deactivated restricted area identity cards that
    have not been retrieved by the operator;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    reported as lost or stolen;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} steps taken to retrieve deactivated restricted area
    identity cards;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} compliance with section 151; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    destroyed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deactivated cards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a record respecting a
  restricted area identity card that has been deactivated must be
  retained for at least one year from the day on which the card was
  deactivated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lost or stolen cards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A record respecting a restricted area identity card
  that has been reported as lost or stolen must be retained for at least
  one year from the card's expiry date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision to Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must provide the
  Minister with the records on reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 17](#1343977-1344914){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343977-1344914.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-163-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 163"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Restricted Area Access Control Process]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776081 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of identity verification system

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must implement and maintain a restricted area access control
process that uses the identity verification system.

#### [Control of Access to Restricted Areas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776084 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unauthorized access prohibition

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** A person must not enter
or remain in a restricted area unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a person to whom a restricted area identity card
  has been issued; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is in possession of a document of entitlement, other
  than a restricted area identity card, for the restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 165"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restricted area identity cards ---
conditions of use

- **[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person to whom a restricted area identity card has been issued must
  not enter or remain in a restricted area unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are acting in the course of their employment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the card is in their possession;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the card is active; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} as applicable, they are in possession of a key that
    has been issued to them for the restricted area, or a combination
    code or personal identification code that has been assigned to them
    for the restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(d) does not apply to crew members.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Display of restricted area identity cards

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person to whom a restricted area identity card has been issued must
  not enter or remain in a restricted area unless they visibly display
  the card on their outer clothing at all times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Display of temporary passes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom a temporary pass has been issued
  must not enter or remain in a restricted area unless they visibly
  display the pass on their outer clothing at all times.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-167-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 167"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oversight

**[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that a person is not allowed to enter or remain in
a restricted area at the aerodrome unless the person is in possession of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an active restricted area identity card that has been
  issued to the person; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a document of entitlement, other than a restricted
  area identity card, for the restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-168-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 168"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Business Continuity Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776113 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Business continuity plans

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must develop and maintain a business
  continuity plan that, at a minimum, sets out how the operator will
  re-establish normal operations and comply with section 168 in the
  event that the operator is unable to use its restricted area access
  control process to comply with that section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must implement its
  business continuity plan and immediately notify the Minister and CATSA
  if the operator discovers that it is unable to use its restricted area
  access control process to comply with section 168.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of delay

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must immediately notify
  the Minister if the operator discovers that it will be unable, for
  more than 24 hours, to use its restricted area access control process
  to comply with section 168.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must make its business
  continuity plan available to the Minister on reasonable notice given
  by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 18](#1343979-1344918){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343979-1344918.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-169-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 169"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Database backup

**[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must regularly back up any database that the operator uses as
part of the identity verification system.

#### [Use of Restricted Area Identity Cards, Keys, Combination Codes and Personal Identification Codes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776125 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General prohibitions

- **[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} lend or give a restricted area identity card or a
    key that has been issued to them to another person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use a restricted area identity card or a key that
    has been issued to them to allow access to a restricted area at an
    aerodrome to another person without authorization from the operator
    of the aerodrome;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} intentionally alter or otherwise modify a
    restricted area identity card or a key unless they are the operator
    of an aerodrome or a person designated by the operator;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} use a restricted area identity card or a key that
    has been issued to another person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} have in their possession, without reasonable
    excuse, a restricted area identity card or a key that has been
    issued to another person;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} use a counterfeit restricted area identity card or
    a counterfeit key; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} make a copy of a restricted area identity card or a
    key.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure or use of codes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person, other than the operator of an aerodrome or
  a person designated by the operator, must not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} disclose a combination code or personal
    identification code; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use another person's combination code or personal
    identification code.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of loss or theft

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person to whom a restricted area identity card or a key has been
  issued must immediately report its loss or theft to their employer or
  to the operator of an aerodrome who issued the card or key.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's duty to report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer who is informed by an employee of the
  loss or theft of a restricted area identity card or a key must
  immediately report the loss or theft to the operator of an aerodrome
  who issued the card or key.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of non-functioning card

**[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** An employer who is
informed by an employee that a restricted area identity card is not
functioning must immediately notify the operator of an aerodrome who
issued the card.

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2014-153, s. 15\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-174-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 174"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Presentation and Surrender of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776148 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation on demand

- **[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person in possession of a restricted area identity card who is in a
  restricted area at an aerodrome must, on demand, present the card to
  the Minister, the operator of the aerodrome, the person's employer or
  a peace officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation during screening

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person in possession of a restricted area identity
  card who is being screened by a screening officer at a restricted area
  access point or at a location in a restricted area must, on demand,
  present the card to the screening officer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surrender on demand

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person in possession of a restricted area identity card must, on
  demand, surrender it to the Minister, the operator of an aerodrome, a
  screening officer or a peace officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand by Minister or operator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister or the operator of an aerodrome may
  demand the surrender of a restricted area identity card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card has expired or has been reported as lost
    or stolen;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the card has been deactivated; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the surrender of the card is required to ensure
    aviation security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand by screening officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A screening officer may demand the surrender of a
  restricted area identity card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card has expired or has been reported as lost
    or stolen;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the card has been deactivated; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the screening officer is carrying out screening at
    a restricted area access point or at a location in a restricted area
    and the person who is in possession of the card refuses to be
    screened or refuses to submit goods in their possession or control
    for screening.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand by peace officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A peace officer may demand the surrender of a
  restricted area identity card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card has expired or has been reported as lost
    or stolen; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is an immediate threat to aviation security,
    the security of any aircraft or aerodrome or other aviation facility
    or the safety of the public, passengers or crew members, and the
    surrender of the card is required to respond to the threat.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of cards

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** A screening officer or
a peace officer to whom a person surrenders a restricted area identity
card must return the card to the operator of the aerodrome where the
card is surrendered or to the operator of an aerodrome who issued the
card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of Minister

**[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome to whom a person surrenders a restricted area identity card
must notify the Minister if the operator demanded the surrender in
accordance with paragraph 176(2)(c).

#### [Escort and Surveillance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776175 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General requirement

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that any person who is in a
  restricted area at the aerodrome and is not in possession of a
  restricted area identity card

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is escorted by a person in possession of an active
    restricted area identity card that has been issued to them; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is kept under surveillance by a person in
    possession of an active restricted area identity card that has been
    issued to them, in the case of an area the limits of which are
    defined for a specific purpose, such as construction or maintenance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of the
  following persons:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} passengers who have been screened; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} inspectors.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Escort ratio

- **[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that at least one escort is
  provided for every 10 persons who require escort.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surveillance ratio

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must ensure that no more
  than 20 persons at a time are kept under surveillance by one person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to remain together

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person under escort must remain with the escort while the person is in
  a restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An escort must remain with the person under escort
  while the person is in a restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to inform

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The person who appoints an escort must inform the
  escort of the requirement to remain with the person under escort while
  that person is in a restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Screening requirement

**[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that a person under escort or surveillance at the
aerodrome and any goods in the person's possession or control are
screened at a screening checkpoint before the person enters a sterile
area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- conveyances

- **[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome is not required to place an escort or
  surveillance personnel in a conveyance that is in a restricted area at
  the aerodrome and is carrying persons who require escort or
  surveillance if the conveyance travels in a convoy with an escort
  conveyance that contains at least one person in possession of an
  active restricted area identity card that has been issued to them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception to exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must ensure that, if a
  person who requires escort or surveillance disembarks from a
  conveyance in a restricted area at the aerodrome, the person is
  escorted or kept under surveillance in accordance with section 180.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Escort conveyances

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that, at the aerodrome, at least one escort
conveyance is provided for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} every three conveyances requiring escort to or from
  an air terminal building apron area for a purpose other than snow
  removal operations;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} every six conveyances requiring escort to or from an
  air terminal building apron area for snow removal operations; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} every six conveyances requiring escort to or from a
  restricted area other than an air terminal building apron area.

#### [Inspectors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776209 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

**[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Division requires an inspector acting in the course of their employment
to be in possession of a restricted area identity card or any other
document issued or approved by the operator of an aerodrome as
authorization for the inspector to enter or remain in a restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspector's credentials

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** The credentials issued
by the Minister to an inspector do not constitute a restricted area
identity card even if the credentials are compatible with the identity
verification system or with an access control system established by the
operator of an aerodrome.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Escort privileges

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Division prohibits an inspector from escorting a person who is in a
restricted area and is not in possession of a restricted area identity
card if the inspector

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is acting in the course of their employment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not escort more than 10 persons at one time;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} remains with the person while the person is in the
  restricted area;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensures that the person remains with the inspector
  while the person is in the restricted area; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} ensures that the person and any goods in their
  possession or control are screened at a screening checkpoint before
  the person enters a sterile area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conveyance escort privileges

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Division prohibits
  an inspector from escorting a person who is in a conveyance in a
  restricted area and is not in possession of a restricted area identity
  card if the inspector

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is acting in the course of their employment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not escort more than 10 persons at one time;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is either in the conveyance or in an escort
    conveyance that is travelling in a convoy with the conveyance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person under escort disembarks from a conveyance
  in a restricted area, the inspector must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} remain with the person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the person remains with the inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person under escort is travelling to or from an
  air terminal building apron area, the Minister must ensure that at
  least one escort conveyance is provided for every three conveyances
  requiring escort in a convoy and that at least one inspector is in
  each escort conveyance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a person under escort is travelling to or from a
  restricted area other than an air terminal building apron area, the
  Minister must ensure that at least one escort conveyance is provided
  for every six conveyances requiring escort in a convoy and that at
  least one inspector is in each escort conveyance.

### [DIVISION 9]{.HLabel2}[Airport Security Programs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776235 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776236 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the regulatory framework for promoting a comprehensive, coordinated and
integrated approach to airport security. The processes required under
this Division are intended to facilitate the establishment and
implementation of effective airport security programs that reflect the
circumstances of each aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-189-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 189"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776241 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Processes and procedures

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
any reference to a process in this Division includes the procedures, if
any, that are necessary to implement that process.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-190-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 190"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Airport Security Program Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776246 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to establish and implement

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must establish and implement an airport
  security program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As part of its airport security program, the operator
  of an aerodrome must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} define and document the aerodrome-related security
    roles and responsibilities assigned to each of the operator's
    employee groups and contractor groups;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicate the information referred to in
    paragraph (a) to the employees and contractors in those groups;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have a security policy statement that establishes
    an overall commitment and direction for aerodrome security and sets
    out the operator's security objectives;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} communicate the security policy statement in an
    accessible manner to all persons who are employed at the aerodrome
    or who require access to the aerodrome in the course of their
    employment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a process for responding to
    aerodrome-related security incidents and breaches in a coordinated
    manner that is intended to minimize their impact;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a security awareness
    program that promotes a culture of security vigilance and awareness
    among the following persons:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} persons who are employed at the aerodrome,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} crew members who are based at the aerodrome, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} persons, other than crew members, who require
      access to the aerodrome in the course of their employment;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} assess risk information and disseminate it within
    the operator's organization for the purpose of informed
    decision-making about aviation security;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a process for receiving,
    retaining, disclosing and disposing of sensitive information
    respecting aviation security in order to protect the information
    from unauthorized access;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} identify sensitive information respecting aviation
    security and receive, retain, disclose and dispose of sensitive
    information respecting aviation security in a manner that protects
    the information from unauthorized access;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} disclose sensitive information respecting aviation
    security to the following persons if they have been assigned
    aerodrome-related security roles and responsibilities and require
    the information to carry out those roles and responsibilities:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} persons who are employed at the aerodrome, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} persons who require access to the aerodrome in
      the course of their employment;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} have a current scale map of the aerodrome that
    identifies all restricted areas, security barriers and restricted
    area access points; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} document how the operator achieves compliance with
    the aviation security provisions of the Act and the regulatory
    requirements that apply to the operator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other program requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following also form part of the airport security
  program:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the security official referred to in section 112;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the aerodrome security personnel training referred
