    to in sections 115 and 116;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the security committee or other working group or
    forum referred to in section 195;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the multi-agency advisory committee referred to in
    section 196;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the airport security risk assessment referred to in
    section 197;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the strategic airport security plan referred to in
    section 202;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the emergency plan referred to in section 206; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the security exercises referred to in sections 207
    and 208.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, ss. 11, 65(F)
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-191-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 191"){.PITLink}
:::

**\[192 reserved\]**

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

- **[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} keep documentation related to its airport security
    risk assessment and any review of it for at least five years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep documentation related to its strategic airport
    security plan and any amendment to it for at least five years; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} keep all other documentation related to its airport
    security program for at least two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must make the
  documentation available to the Minister on reasonable notice given by
  the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 193"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to amend

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must amend its airport security program if the operator
identifies, at the aerodrome, an aviation security risk that is not
addressed by the program.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-194-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 194"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Committees]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776296 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security committee

- **[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must have a security committee or other
  working group or forum that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} advises the operator on the development of controls
    and processes that are necessary at the aerodrome in order to comply
    with the aviation security provisions of the Act and the regulatory
    requirements that apply to the operator;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} helps coordinate the implementation of the controls
    and processes that are necessary at the aerodrome in order to comply
    with the aviation security provisions of the Act and the regulatory
    requirements that apply to the operator; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} promotes the sharing of information respecting the
    airport security program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must manage the
  security committee or other working group or forum in accordance with
  written terms of reference that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} identify its membership; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} define the roles and responsibilities of each
    member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must keep records of
  the activities and decisions of the security committee or other
  working group or forum.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-195-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 195"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multi-agency advisory committee

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must have a multi-agency advisory committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must invite at least
  the following persons and organizations to be members of the
  multi-agency advisory committee:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Department of Transport;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} CATSA;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the police service with jurisdiction at the
    aerodrome;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Royal Canadian Mounted Police;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the Canadian Security Intelligence Service; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the Canada Border Services Agency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of reference

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must manage the
  multi-agency advisory committee in accordance with written terms of
  reference.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objectives

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The objectives of the multi-agency advisory committee
  are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to advise the operator of the aerodrome on its
    airport security risk assessment and its strategic airport security
    plan; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to promote the sharing of sensitive information
    respecting aviation security at the aerodrome.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must keep records of
  the activities and decisions of the multi-agency advisory committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

#### [Airport Security Risk Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776332 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Airport security risk assessments

**[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must have an airport security risk assessment that identifies,
assesses and prioritizes aviation security risks and that includes the
following elements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a threat assessment that evaluates the probability
  that aviation security incidents will occur at the aerodrome;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a criticality assessment that prioritizes the areas,
  assets, infrastructure and operations at or associated with the
  aerodrome that most require protection from acts and attempted acts of
  unlawful interference with civil aviation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a vulnerability assessment that considers the extent
  to which the areas, assets, infrastructure and operations at or
  associated with the aerodrome are susceptible to loss or damage and
  that evaluates this susceptibility in the context of the threat
  assessment; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an impact assessment that, at a minimum, measures the
  consequences of an aviation security incident or potential aviation
  security incident in terms of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a decrease in public safety and security,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} financial and economic loss, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a loss of public confidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission for approval

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must submit its airport security risk assessment to the
Minister for approval, and must submit a new airport security risk
assessment to the Minister within five years after the date of the most
recent approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to consult

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must consult its multi-agency advisory committee when the
operator is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} preparing its airport security risk assessment for
  submission to the Minister for approval; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} conducting a review of its airport security risk
  assessment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Airport security risk assessment ---
annual review

- **[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must conduct a review of its airport security
  risk assessment at least once a year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Airport security risk assessment ---
  other reviews

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must also conduct a
  review of its airport security risk assessment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a special event that is scheduled to take place at
    the aerodrome could affect aerodrome security;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator is planning a change to the physical
    layout or operation of the aerodrome that could affect aviation
    security at the aerodrome;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an environmental or operational change at the
    aerodrome could affect aerodrome security;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a change in regulatory requirements could affect
    aerodrome security;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operator identifies, at the aerodrome, a
    vulnerability that is not addressed in the assessment, or the
    Minister identifies such a vulnerability to the operator; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that there is a
    change in the threat environment that could result in a new or
    unaddressed medium to high risk.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equivalency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a review conducted under
  subsection (2) counts as a review required under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When the operator of the aerodrome conducts a review
  of its airport security risk assessment, the operator must document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any decision to amend or to not amend the
    assessment or the operator's risk-management strategy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for that decision; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the factors that were taken into consideration in
    making that decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must notify the
  Minister if, as a result of a review of its airport security risk
  assessment, the operator amends the assessment

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to include a new medium to high risk; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to raise or lower the level of a risk within the
    medium to high range.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must
approve an airport security risk assessment submitted by the operator of
an aerodrome if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assessment meets the requirements of section 197;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the assessment has been reviewed by an executive
  within the operator's organization who is responsible for security;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operator has considered risk information provided
  by its multi-agency advisory committee;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the operator has considered all available and
  relevant information; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operator has not overlooked an aviation security
  risk that could affect the operation of the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

#### [Strategic Airport Security Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776387 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Strategic airport security plans

- **[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must establish a strategic airport security
  plan that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} summarizes the operator's strategy to prepare for,
    detect, prevent, respond to and recover from acts or attempted acts
    of unlawful interference with civil aviation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes a risk-management strategy that addresses
    the medium to high aviation security risks identified and
    prioritized in the operator's airport security risk assessment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sets out a menu of additional safeguards that are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} intended to mitigate heightened risk conditions
      in a graduated manner, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} consistent with the operator's legal powers and
      obligations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Menu of additional safeguards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The menu of additional safeguards must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} describe, by activity type and location, the
    safeguards in place at the aerodrome in respect of AVSEC level 1
    operating conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow the rapid selection of additional safeguards
    by activity type or location; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} indicate the persons and organizations responsible
    for implementing each additional safeguard.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Activity types

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (2)(a) and (b), the
  activity types must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} access controls;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} monitoring and patrolling;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} communications; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} other operational controls.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Locations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (2)(a) and (b), the
  locations must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} public areas of the aerodrome;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} areas of the aerodrome that are not public areas
    but are not restricted areas; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} restricted areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to consult

**[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must consult its multi-agency advisory committee when the
operator

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishes its strategic airport security plan; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} amends its strategic airport security plan under
  subsection 205.2(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to submit

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must submit its strategic airport security plan to the
Minister for approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to implement

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must, as soon as its strategic airport security plan is
approved, implement its risk-management strategy.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of plan

**[[205.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.1 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must
approve a strategic airport security plan submitted by the operator of
an aerodrome if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plan meets the requirements of section 202;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the plan has been reviewed by an executive within the
  operator's organization who is responsible for security;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the plan is likely to enable the operator to prepare
  for, detect, prevent, respond to and recover from acts or attempted
  acts of unlawful interference with civil aviation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the risk-management strategy is in proportion to the
  risks it addresses;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operator has considered the advice of its
  multi-agency advisory committee;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the operator has not overlooked an aviation security
  risk that could affect the operation of the aerodrome;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the additional safeguards can be implemented rapidly
  and consistently;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the additional safeguards are consistent with
  existing rights and freedoms; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the plan can be implemented without compromising
  aviation security.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments

- **[[205.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may
  amend its strategic airport security plan at any time, but must do so
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plan does not reflect the operator's most
    recent airport security risk assessment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that there is a
    change in the threat environment that could result in a new or
    unaddressed medium to high risk;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that there is a
    change in the threat environment that requires the addition or
    deletion of additional safeguards;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that its
    risk-management strategy is not in proportion to a medium to high
    risk set out in the operator's airport security risk assessment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operator identifies a deficiency in the plan;
    or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a change is made in the aviation security
    provisions of the Act or in regulatory requirements and the change
    affects the additional safeguards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its strategic
  airport security plan, the operator must document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reason for the amendment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the factors that were taken into consideration in
    making that amendment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of amendment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its strategic
  airport security plan, the operator must, as soon as possible, submit
  the amendment to the Minister for approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must approve an amendment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an amendment to the summary required
    under paragraph 202(1)(a), the conditions set out in
    paragraphs 205.1(a) to (c) have been met;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an amendment to the risk-management
    strategy required under paragraph 202(1)(b), the conditions set out
    in paragraphs 205.1(a) to (f) and (i) have been met; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an amendment to the menu of
    additional safeguards required under paragraph 202(1)(c), the
    conditions set out in paragraphs 205.1(a), (b) and (f) to (i) have
    been met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its
  risk-management strategy, the operator must implement the amended
  version of the strategy once it is approved by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

#### [Emergency Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776465 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plan requirements

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must establish an emergency plan that sets
  out the response procedures to be followed at the aerodrome for
  coordinated responses to the following emergencies:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} bomb threats;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} hijackings of aircraft; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} other acts of unlawful interference with civil
    aviation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Response procedures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The response procedures must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out in detail the actions to be taken by the
    employees and contractors of the operator of the aerodrome and
    identify the responsibilities of all other persons or organizations
    involved, including, as applicable, the police, emergency response
    providers, air carriers, emergency coordination centre personnel and
    control tower or flight service station personnel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the evacuation of
    air terminal buildings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the search of air
    terminal buildings;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the handling and
    disposal of a suspected bomb; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the detention on
    the ground of any aircraft involved in a bomb threat or hijacking.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 12
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Exercises]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776481 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operations-based security exercise

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must, at least once every two years, carry
  out an operations-based security exercise that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of the operator's emergency
    plan in response to an act of unlawful interference with civil
    aviation and involves the persons and organizations referred to in
    the plan; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of additional safeguards
    that the operator chooses from its menu of additional safeguards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equivalency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, in response to an aviation security incident, the
  Minister raises the AVSEC level for an aerodrome or any part of an
  aerodrome, the implementation of additional safeguards by the operator
  of the aerodrome counts as an operations-based security exercise for
  the purposes of subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 12
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discussion-based security exercise

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must, at least once a year, carry out a
  discussion-based security exercise that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of the operator's emergency
    plan in response to an act of unlawful interference with civil
    aviation and involves the persons and organizations referred to in
    the plan; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of additional safeguards
    that the operator chooses from its menu of additional safeguards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the operator of an aerodrome
  is not required to carry out a discussion-based security exercise in
  any year in which it carries out an operations-based security
  exercise.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 12
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-208-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 208"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

**[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must give the Minister 60 days' notice of any security
exercise that the operator plans to carry out.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

#### [Records]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776504 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional safeguards

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  time additional safeguards are implemented at an aerodrome in order to
  mitigate heightened risk conditions related to aviation security, the
  operator of the aerodrome must create a record that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the additional safeguards that
    were implemented;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an evaluation of the effectiveness of those
    additional safeguards; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of any actions that are planned in
    order to address deficiencies identified during the implementation
    of those additional safeguards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergencies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each time an emergency referred to in
  subsection 206(1) occurs at an aerodrome, the operator of the
  aerodrome must create a record that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the emergency;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an evaluation of the effectiveness of the
    operator's emergency plan; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of any actions that are planned in
    order to address deficiencies identified during the emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercises

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each time a security exercise is carried out at an
  aerodrome, the operator of the aerodrome must create a record that
  includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an outline of the exercise scenario;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an evaluation of the effectiveness of the exercise;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of any actions that are planned in
    order to address deficiencies identified during the exercise.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 12
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-210-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 210"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Corrective Actions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776523 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective actions

**[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 212,
the operator of an aerodrome must immediately take corrective actions to
address a vulnerability that contributes to a heightened aviation
security risk at the aerodrome and that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is identified to the operator by the Minister; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is identified by the operator.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-211-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 211"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective action plan

**[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** If a corrective action
to be taken by the operator of an aerodrome under section 211 involves a
phased approach, the operator must include in its airport security
program a corrective action plan that sets out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the vulnerability to be addressed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a rationale for the phased approach; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a timetable setting out when each phase of the
  corrective action plan will be completed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-212-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 212"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Disclosure of Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776537 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** A person other than the
Minister must not disclose security-sensitive information that is
created or used under this Division unless the disclosure is required by
law or is necessary to comply or facilitate compliance with the aviation
security provisions of the Act, regulatory requirements or the
requirements of an emergency direction.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-213-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 213"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 10]{.HLabel2}[Reserved]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776542 .Subheading}

**\[214 to 223 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 11]{.HLabel2}[Primary Security Line Partners]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776544 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776545 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the role of a primary security line partner in supporting the
establishment and implementation of an effective airport security
program by the operator of an aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-224-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 224"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Official]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776550 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** A security official of
a primary security line partner at an aerodrome is an individual who is
responsible for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} coordinating and overseeing compliance with the
  regulatory requirements that apply to the partner under this Part; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} acting as the principal contact between the partner,
  the operator of the aerodrome and the Minister with respect to
  security matters, including compliance with the regulatory
  requirements that apply to the partner under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-225-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 225"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One security official at all times

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  primary security line partner at an aerodrome must have, at all times,
  at least one security official or acting security official.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contact information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The primary security line partner must provide the
  operator of the aerodrome and the Minister with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of each security official and acting
    security official; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 24-hour contact information for those officials.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

#### [Support for Airport Security Programs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776566 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** At each aerodrome where
a primary security line partner carries out operations, the partner must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} define and document the aerodrome-related security
  roles and responsibilities assigned to each of the partner's employee
  groups and contractor groups that require access to restricted areas
  at the aerodrome in the course of their employment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicate the information referred to in
  paragraph (a) to the employees and contractors in those groups and
  document how that information is communicated;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} establish, implement and document a security
  awareness program that promotes a culture of security vigilance and
  awareness among its employees and contractors if the security
  awareness program of the operator of the aerodrome does not cover
  matters that are unique to the partner's operations;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} document the measures, procedures and processes that
  the partner has in place at the aerodrome to protect the security of
  restricted areas and to prevent breaches of the primary security line;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} create a document that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} describes each area on the aerodrome's primary
    security line that is occupied by the partner,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} indicates the location of each restricted area
    access point in those areas, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} describes those restricted area access points;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} establish, implement and document a process for
  receiving, retaining, disclosing and disposing of sensitive
  information respecting aerodrome security in order to protect the
  information from unauthorized access; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} identify sensitive information respecting aviation
  security and receive, retain, disclose and dispose of sensitive
  information respecting aerodrome security in a manner that protects
  the information from unauthorized access.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

**\[228 to 230 reserved\]**

#### [Provision of Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776582 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of information to operator of
aerodrome

- **[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  each aerodrome where a primary security line partner carries out
  operations, the partner must provide the operator of the aerodrome
  with the information that is documented or created under this Division
  on reasonable notice given by the operator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision to Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The primary security line partner must provide the
  Minister with the same information on reasonable notice given by the
  Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-231-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 231"){.PITLink}
:::

**\[232 and 233 reserved\]**

#### [Corrective Actions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776591 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective actions

- **[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 235, a primary
  security line partner must immediately take corrective actions to
  address a vulnerability that contributes to an aerodrome-related
  security risk and that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is identified to the partner by the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is identified to the partner by the operator of the
    aerodrome where the partner carries out operations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is identified by the partner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a primary security line partner takes corrective
  actions at an aerodrome, the primary security line partner must
  immediately notify the operator of the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-234-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 234"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective action plan

**[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** If a corrective action
to be taken by a primary security line partner under section 234
involves a phased approach, the primary security line partner must
provide the Minister and the operator of the aerodrome with a corrective
action plan that sets out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the vulnerability to be addressed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a rationale for the phased approach; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a timetable setting out when each phase of the
  corrective action plan will be completed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-235-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 235"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Disclosure of Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776609 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[235.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235.1 .sectionLabel}** A person other than
the Minister must not disclose security-sensitive information that is
created or used under this Division unless the disclosure is required by
law or is necessary to comply or facilitate compliance with the aviation
security provisions of the Act, regulatory requirements or the
requirements of an emergency direction.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 16
:::

### [DIVISION 12]{.HLabel2}[Other Aerodrome Operations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776614 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776615 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting aerodrome operations that are not dealt with in
any other Division of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 13
:::

#### [Construction Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776620 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to notify Minister

- **[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must notify the Minister of all plans to
  begin new construction or to make a change to the physical security of
  the aerodrome, if the construction or change relates to regulatory
  requirements respecting passengers, aircraft, baggage, cargo or mail.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} state the date on which the construction will begin
    or the change will be made; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out a description of the construction or change
    and the safeguards that will be implemented to maintain security in
    the areas of the aerodrome that will be affected by the construction
    activities.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 13
:::

**[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2019-183, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-238-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 238"){.PITLink}
:::

**\[239 to 245 reserved\]**

## [PART 5]{.HLabel1}[Class 2 Aerodromes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-776640 .Part}

### [Overview]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776641 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Part overview

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out the
basic regulatory framework for security at aerodromes listed in
Schedule 2.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776644 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies in
respect of aerodromes listed in Schedule 2.

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Prohibited Items]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776647 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776648 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** This Division completes
and supplements the regulatory framework set out in Part 3.

#### [Authorization for Carriage of or Access to Explosive Substances and Incendiary Devices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776651 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

**[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome may authorize a person to carry or have access to an explosive
substance or an incendiary device at the aerodrome if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosive substance or incendiary device is to be
  used at the aerodrome

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for excavation, demolition or construction work,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in fireworks displays,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} by persons operating explosives detection
    equipment or handling explosives detection dogs,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} by a police service, or

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} by military personnel; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator has reasonable grounds to believe that
  the safety of the aerodrome and the safety of persons and aircraft at
  the aerodrome will not be jeopardized by the presence of the explosive
  substance or incendiary device.

#### [Availability of Prohibited Items]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776661 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- sterile area

- **[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must not permit goods listed or described in
  the general list of prohibited items or, as applicable, the specific
  list of prohibited items to be made available to persons in a sterile
  area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- liquids, aerosols and
  gels

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of liquids,
  aerosols and gels that are made available to persons in accordance
  with a security measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- knives

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of rounded,
  dull-blade knives and plastic knives that are made available to the
  customers of a concessionaire with the permission of the operator of
  the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 14
:::

**\[251 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Threats and Incidents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776672 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776673 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the regulatory framework for dealing with threats and incidents at an
aerodrome.

#### [Threat Response]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776676 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Area under operator's control

**[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who is made aware of a threat against an aviation facility, or
a part of the aerodrome, that is under the operator's control must
immediately determine whether there is a threat that jeopardizes the
security of the facility or that part of the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137834){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137834.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-253-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 253"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Area under control of other person

**[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who is made aware of a threat against an aviation facility, or
a part of the aerodrome, that is under the control of a person carrying
on any activity at the aerodrome, other than the operator, must
immediately

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} notify the person of the nature of the threat; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} determine whether there is a threat that jeopardizes
  the security of the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137837){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137837.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-254-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 254"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Threats

**[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who determines that there is a threat that jeopardizes the
security of the aerodrome must immediately take all of the measures
necessary to ensure the safety of the aerodrome and persons at the
aerodrome, including informing the appropriate police service of the
nature of the threat.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137841){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137841.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-255-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 255"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of other person

**[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** A person, other than a
screening authority, who is carrying on any activity at an aerodrome and
who is made aware of a threat against the aerodrome must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately notify the operator of the aerodrome of
  the nature of the threat; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} assist the operator of the aerodrome in determining
  whether there is a threat that jeopardizes the security of the
  aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137844){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137844.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-256-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 256"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Threats identified by other person

**[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** If it is determined
under paragraph 15(b), 254(b) or 256(b) that there is a threat that
jeopardizes the security of an aerodrome, the operator of the aerodrome
must immediately take all of the measures necessary to ensure the safety
of the aerodrome and persons at the aerodrome, including informing the
appropriate police service of the nature of the threat.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137848){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137848.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-257-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 257"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Information Reporting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776691 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security incidents

**[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must immediately notify the Minister when any of the following
incidents occur:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the discovery, at the aerodrome, of a weapon,
  explosive substance or incendiary device that is not permitted under
  subsection 78(2);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an explosion at the aerodrome, unless the explosion
  is known to be the result of an accident, excavation, demolition or
  construction work, or the use of fireworks displays;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a threat against the aerodrome; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an aviation security incident that involves a peace
  officer anywhere at the aerodrome other than areas under an air
  carrier's control.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-149, s. 3](#1130155-1137851){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130155-1137851.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-258-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 258"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commercial air service information

**[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must provide the Minister with written notice of any new
commercial air service that is to begin at an air terminal building.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[AVSEC Levels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776700 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776701 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting the implementation of additional safeguards in
the event of heightened risk conditions.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 17
:::

#### [AVSEC Level Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776706 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional safeguards

**[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** If the AVSEC level is
raised or maintained above level 1 for an aerodrome or any part of an
aerodrome, the operator of the aerodrome must immediately take the
following actions:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine which additional safeguards are likely to
  mitigate the heightened risk condition;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify any persons or organizations that have
  aviation security roles and responsibilities at the aerodrome and are
  affected by the heightened risk condition;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} implement or continue to implement the additional
  safeguards; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} notify the Minister of the additional safeguards that
  are being or will be implemented.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 17
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

**[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** When the AVSEC level is
lowered for an aerodrome or any part of an aerodrome, the operator of
the aerodrome must immediately notify the persons and organizations that
were notified under paragraph 261(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 17
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Legal powers and obligations

**[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
nothing in these Regulations authorizes the operator of an aerodrome to
implement additional safeguards that are inconsistent with the
operator's legal powers and obligations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 17
:::

**\[264 and 265 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Personnel and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776724 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776725 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting aerodrome security personnel and other persons
who are assigned aerodrome-related security roles and responsibilities
at an aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 75
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-266-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 266"){.PITLink}
:::

**\[267 and 268 reserved\]**

#### [Security Official]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776731 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-153, s. 18

\]
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** A security official of
an aerodrome is an individual who is responsible for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} coordinating and overseeing security controls and
  procedures at the aerodrome; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} acting as the principal contact between the operator
  of the aerodrome and the Minister with respect to security matters,
  including the airport security program.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement

- **[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must have, at all times, at least one
  security official or acting security official.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contact information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must provide the
  Minister with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of each security official and acting
    security official; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 24-hour contact information for those officials.

#### [Aerodrome Security Personnel]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776745 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial training

- **[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that a member of the aerodrome
  security personnel does not carry out an aerodrome-related security
  role or responsibility at the aerodrome unless the member has received
  initial training in relation to that role or responsibility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training elements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Initial training for aerodrome security personnel
  must include instruction and evaluation in relation to the topics set
  out below that are relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles
  and responsibilities of the personnel:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} international instruments respecting aviation
    security, the aviation security provisions of the Act and regulatory
    requirements;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the security controls and procedures at the
    aerodrome where the personnel are employed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} systems and equipment at the aerodrome;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an overview of threats to aviation security and
    acts or attempted acts of unlawful interference with civil aviation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of goods that are listed or
    described in TP 14628 or that pose an immediate threat to aviation
    security; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the actions to be taken by the personnel in
    response to a threat to aviation security or an act or attempted act
    of unlawful interference with civil aviation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grandfathering

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Aerodrome security personnel who are employed at the
  aerodrome on the day on which this section comes into force are
  exempted from initial training in relation to any topic for which they
  have already received training.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Follow-up training

- **[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that aerodrome security personnel
  receive follow-up training when any of the following circumstances
  arise:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a change is made in the aviation security
    provisions of the Act or in regulatory requirements and the change
    is relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles and
    responsibilities of the personnel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a change is made in the security controls and
    procedures at the aerodrome where the personnel are employed and the
    change is relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles and
    responsibilities of the personnel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a new or modified action is to be taken by the
    personnel in response to a threat to aviation security or an act or
    attempted act of unlawful interference with civil aviation; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a significant risk or an emerging trend in aviation
    security is identified to the operator by the Minister and the risk
    or trend is relevant to the aerodrome-related security roles and
    responsibilities of the personnel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Follow-up training

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must ensure that a
  member of the aerodrome security personnel receives follow-up training
  when the Minister or the operator identifies a shortcoming in the
  member's performance when the member is carrying out security controls
  or following security procedures at the aerodrome.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training elements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Follow-up training must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a review of any initial-training element related to
    the circumstance set out in subsection (1) or (2) that gave rise to
    the follow-up training; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} instruction and evaluation in relation to that
    circumstance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}On-the-job training

**[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** If, at an aerodrome,
the initial or follow-up training of aerodrome security personnel
includes on-the-job training, the operator of the aerodrome must ensure
that the on-the-job training is provided by a person who has received
that same training or has significant experience working as a member of
the aerodrome security personnel at an aerodrome listed in Schedule 1 or
2.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training records

- **[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that, for each individual who
  receives training in accordance with section 271 or 272, there is a
  training record that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the individual's employee group or contractor
    group, if applicable, and a description of the individual's
    aerodrome-related security roles and responsibilities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of all the training that the
    individual has received in accordance with section 271 or 272; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} evaluation results for all the training that the
    individual has received in accordance with section 271 or 272.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record keeping

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must keep the training
  record for at least two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must make the training
  record available to the Minister on reasonable notice given by the
  Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 19
:::

**\[275 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Facilitation of Screening]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776793 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776794 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting the facilitation of screening operations at an
aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 15
:::

#### [Screening of Passengers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776799 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passenger screening facilities

**[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for passenger screening
checkpoints and must make at least one facility available for the
private screening of passengers.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 15
- SOR/2014-153, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-277-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 277"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False declaration notice

- **[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must post a notice at each passenger
  screening checkpoint stating that it is an offence for a person at the
  aerodrome to falsely declare

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the person is carrying a weapon, an explosive
    substance, an incendiary device or any other item that could be used
    to jeopardize the security of an aerodrome or aircraft, or that such
    an item is contained in goods in the person's possession or control
    or in goods that the person has tendered or is tendering for
    screening or transportation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that another person who is at an aerodrome or on
    board an aircraft is carrying a weapon, an explosive substance, an
    incendiary device or any other item that could be used to jeopardize
    the security of an aerodrome or aircraft, or that such an item is
    contained in goods in the other person's possession or control or in
    goods that the other person has tendered or is tendering for
    screening or transportation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice must be clearly visible and be in at least
  both official languages.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 15
- SOR/2014-153, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-278-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 278"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Screening of Non-passengers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776813 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-passenger screening facilities

**[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for non-passenger screening
checkpoints at restricted area access points and at locations inside
restricted areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-passenger access to restricted areas

**[[279.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279.1 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must, in accordance with a security measure, ensure that
non-passengers enter a restricted area at the aerodrome only through a
restricted area access point where screening is carried out.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-161, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- liquids, aerosols or gels

**[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that non-passengers who access sterile areas are
notified of any restrictions on the possession of liquids, aerosols or
gels in sterile areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 15
:::

#### [Screening of Checked Baggage]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776826 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Checked baggage screening facilities

**[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for the screening of checked
baggage and baggage intended to be checked baggage.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 15
- SOR/2014-153, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-281-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 281"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Baggage Handling Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776831 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No change without agreement

**[[282]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282 .sectionLabel}** If the operator of an
aerodrome is responsible for a baggage handling system, the operator
must not make any change to the system that may affect screening
operations unless the change is agreed to by CATSA.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 15
:::

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Access Controls]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776836 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776837 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[283]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-283 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the regulatory framework for the protection of security-sensitive areas
of aerodromes.

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776840 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sign requirements

- **[[284]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-284 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must post signs on the outside of each
  restricted area access point and each security barrier. Each sign must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in at least both official languages;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the restricted area as a restricted area;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} state that access to the area is restricted to
    authorized persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signs on security barriers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The signs posted on a security barrier must be no
  more than 150 m apart.

#### [Restricted Area Access Points]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776849 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access control system

**[[285]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-285 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that each restricted area access point that allows
access from a non-restricted area to a restricted area has an access
control system consisting of one or more of the following elements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} surveillance by a person authorized by the operator
  of the aerodrome to control access to the restricted area;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} manual locking equipment; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} automated access control equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passenger loading bridge

**[[286]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-286 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that each restricted area access point that is
located between an air terminal building and a passenger loading bridge
has a door that can be locked.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[287]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-287 .sectionLabel}** A person must not enter
a restricted area at an aerodrome except through a restricted area
access point.

#### [Baggage Handling Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776863 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prevention of unauthorized access

**[[288]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-288 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must take measures to prevent unauthorized access to a baggage
handling system that is in a restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 17
:::

#### [Doors, Gates, Emergency Exits and Other Devices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776868 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to close and lock --- operators

- **[[289]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-289 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must close and lock any door, gate or other
  device, other than an emergency exit, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator has control of and responsibility for
    the door, gate or other device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door, gate or other device allows access
    between a restricted area and a non-restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exit system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must institute a system,
  on or near an emergency exit, that prevents access by unauthorized
  persons to a restricted area, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator has control of and responsibility for
    the emergency exit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emergency exit allows access between a
    restricted area and a non-restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to close and lock --- partners and
lessees

- **[[290]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-290 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  primary security line partner, or a lessee other than a primary
  security line partner, at an aerodrome must close and lock any door,
  gate or other device, other than an emergency exit, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the partner or lessee has control of and
    responsibility for the door, gate or other device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door, gate or other device allows access
    between a restricted area and a non-restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exit system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A primary security line partner who occupies an area
  on an aerodrome's primary security line must institute a system, on or
  near an emergency exit, that prevents access by unauthorized persons
  to a restricted area, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the partner has control of and responsibility for
    the emergency exit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emergency exit allows access between a
    restricted area and a non-restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary use or control

**[[291]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-291 .sectionLabel}** Any person at an
aerodrome who has temporary use or control of a door, gate or other
device that allows access between a restricted area and a non-restricted
area must prevent access to or from the restricted area by unauthorized
persons.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Uncontrolled restricted area access point

**[[292]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-292 .sectionLabel}** Unless an authorized
person is controlling access between a restricted area and a
non-restricted area at an aerodrome, a person who enters or leaves the
restricted area must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} lock the door, gate or other device that allows
  access to or from the restricted area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent access to or from the restricted area by
  unauthorized persons while the door, gate or other device is open or
  unlocked.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preventing locking

**[[293]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-293 .sectionLabel}** A person at an
aerodrome must not prevent a door, gate or other device, other than an
emergency exit, that allows access between a restricted area and a
non-restricted area from being locked.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exits

**[[294]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-294 .sectionLabel}** A person at an
aerodrome must not open any door that is designated as an emergency exit
and that is also a restricted area access point unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is authorized by the operator of the
  aerodrome to open it; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is an emergency.

#### [Unauthorized Access]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776899 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[295]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-295 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person has been given notice, orally, in writing or by a sign, that
  access to a part of an aerodrome is prohibited or is limited to
  authorized persons, the person must not enter or remain in that part
  of the aerodrome without authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restricted areas

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may authorize a person
  to enter or remain in a restricted area if the requirements of
  Divisions 6 to 8 are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public areas other than restricted
  areas

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may authorize a person
  to enter or remain in a part of the aerodrome that is not a public
  area but is not a restricted area if the safety of the aerodrome,
  persons at the aerodrome and aircraft is not jeopardized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public areas other than restricted
  areas

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A lessee at an aerodrome who has the use of, or is
  responsible for, a part of the aerodrome that is not a public area but
  is not a restricted area may authorize a person to enter or remain in
  that part of the aerodrome if the safety of the aerodrome, persons at
  the aerodrome and aircraft is not jeopardized.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-295-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 295"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Inspectors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776911 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to allow access

**[[296]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-296 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must allow an inspector to enter or remain in a restricted
area if the inspector is acting in the course of their employment and
presents their credentials.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 18
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Documents of Entitlement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776916 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[297]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-297 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
provisions respecting documents of entitlement. Requirements respecting
the issue and use of restricted area identity card are set out in
Division 8.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of documents

- **[[298]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-298 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Only
  the following documents are documents of entitlement at an aerodrome:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a restricted area identity card;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a temporary pass issued by the operator of the
    aerodrome;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a boarding pass, a ticket, or any other document
    accepted by an air carrier that confirms the status of the person to
    whom it was issued as a passenger on a flight and that is approved
    by the operator of the aerodrome;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a passenger escort form that is approved by the
    operator of the aerodrome;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a courtesy-lounge or conference-room pass that is
    issued by an air carrier and that is approved by the operator of the
    aerodrome; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a document that is issued or approved by the
    operator of the aerodrome in accordance with a security measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pilot's licence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A pilot's licence issued under the [Canadian Aviation
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-96-433) is a document of entitlement
  for a restricted area that is used by general aviation, if the holder
  of the licence also holds a valid medical certificate of a category
  that is appropriate for that licence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is acting in the course of their employment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requires access to an aircraft that they own or
    operate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 19
- SOR/2014-153, s. 23
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-298-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 298"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Enhanced Access Controls]{.HTitleText2} {#h-776936 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776937 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[299]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-299 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
enhanced access control requirements, including requirements respecting
the identity verification system referred to in section 56.

#### [Identity Verification System]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776940 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information

- **[[300]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-300 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome is authorized to disclose to the Minister or
  CATSA any information that is necessary for the proper operation of
  the identity verification system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the operator of an aerodrome
  must not disclose to CATSA the identity of an applicant for a
  restricted area identity card or the identity of a person to whom a
  restricted area identity card has been issued unless the operator
  grants CATSA access to its databases to maintain or repair the
  identity verification system and CATSA's access to the person's
  identity is incidental to the maintenance or repairs.

#### [Information To Be Displayed on a Restricted Area Identity Card]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776946 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required information

- **[[301]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-301 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must ensure that the following information is
  displayed on each restricted area identity card that it issues:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the full name of the person to whom the card is
    issued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a photograph depicting a frontal view of the
    person's face;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the expiry date of the card;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the aerodrome where the card is issued;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person's employer, if the person
    has a single employer;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the terms "multi-employer" and "employeur
    multiple", if the person has more than one employer.

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiration date

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A restricted area identity card, including one that
  is issued to a person who requires access to restricted areas at more
  than one aerodrome, expires no later than five years after the day on
  which it is issued or on the day on which the security clearance of
  the person to whom the card is issued expires, whichever is earlier.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must ensure that all
  information that is displayed on a restricted area identity card is in
  both official languages.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 19](#1343981-1344927){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343981-1344927.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-301-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 301"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Issuance of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-776965 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance criteria

- **[[302]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-302 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must not issue a restricted area identity
  card to a person unless the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applies in writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is sponsored in writing by their employer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has a security clearance;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} consents in writing to the collection, use,
    retention, disclosure and destruction of information for the
    purposes of this Division; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} confirms that the information displayed on the card
    is correct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Activation requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must not issue a
  restricted area identity card to a person unless the card has been
  activated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False information

**[[303]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-303 .sectionLabel}** A person must not
provide false information for the purpose of obtaining a restricted area
identity card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sponsorship

**[[304]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-304 .sectionLabel}** An employer must not

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} sponsor an employee who does not require ongoing
  access to restricted areas in the course of their employment; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} knowingly sponsor an employee for more than one
  restricted area identity card at a time.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of multiple cards

**[[305]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-305 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must not issue more than one restricted area identity card at
a time to a person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement of cards

**[[306]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-306 .sectionLabel}** Before replacing a
lost, stolen or non-functional restricted area identity card, the
operator of an aerodrome must ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person applying for the replacement card is the
  person to whom the lost, stolen or non-functional card has been
  issued; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person still has a security clearance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to inform

**[[307]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-307 .sectionLabel}** Before collecting
information from an applicant under this Division, the operator of an
aerodrome must bring to the applicant's attention the purposes for which
the information is collected and the manner in which the information
will be used, retained, disclosed and destroyed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection of information

- **[[308]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-308 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of creating a restricted area identity card for an
  applicant, the operator of an aerodrome must collect the following
  information from the applicant:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's full name;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's height;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a photograph depicting a frontal view of the
    applicant's face;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's fingerprint images and iris images;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the applicant's employer; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's occupation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of images and templates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must, immediately after
  issuing the restricted area identity card, destroy all fingerprint
  images and iris images that the operator collected from the applicant
  and any biometric template created from those images that is not
  stored on the card.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quality control

**[[309]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-309 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
allowing CATSA to monitor the quality of biometric templates and
determining if a restricted area identity card is already active in
respect of an applicant, the operator of an aerodrome must, before
issuing the card, disclose to CATSA any biometric templates created from
the fingerprint images and iris images collected from the applicant.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information

**[[310]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-310 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must take appropriate measures to protect information that is
collected, used, retained or disclosed in accordance with this Division
from loss or theft and from unauthorized access, use, disclosure,
duplication or alteration.

#### [Deactivation of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777005 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deactivation request

- **[[311]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-311 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome who has issued a restricted area identity
  card must immediately ask CATSA to deactivate the card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card expires;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom the card has been issued or
    their employer informs the operator that the card is lost, stolen or
    no longer functional; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom the card has been issued fails,
    on demand, to present or surrender the card to a screening officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reason for deactivation

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of an aerodrome asks CATSA to
  deactivate a restricted area identity card, the operator must inform
  CATSA of the reason for the request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must not ask CATSA to
  deactivate a restricted area identity card for a reason other than a
  reason set out in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of Minister

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must notify the Minister
  if the operator asks CATSA to deactivate a restricted area identity
  card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-311-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 311"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in employment

**[[312]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-312 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome who has issued a restricted area identity card must notify the
Minister immediately if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has a single employer,
  the person to whom the card has been issued ceases to be employed or
  no longer requires ongoing access to restricted areas in the course of
  their employment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has more than one
  employer, the person to whom the card has been issued ceases to be
  employed by all of their employers or no longer requires ongoing
  access to restricted areas in the course of their employment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of employer

**[[313]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-313 .sectionLabel}** The employer of a
person to whom a restricted area identity card has been issued must
immediately notify the operator of an aerodrome who issued the card if
the person ceases to be an employee or no longer requires ongoing access
to restricted areas in the course of their employment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retrieval of cards

- **[[314]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-314 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome who has issued a restricted area identity
  card must take reasonable steps to retrieve the card if it has been
  deactivated and must notify CATSA if the card is not retrieved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of cards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a restricted area identity card has been
  deactivated, the person to whom the card has been issued must
  immediately return it to the operator of an aerodrome who issued it
  unless the card was surrendered in accordance with this Division or
  was lost or stolen.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of cards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must, as soon as
  feasible, destroy a restricted area identity card that has been
  retrieved or returned.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 20](#1343987-1344931){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343987-1344931.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-314-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 314"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Keys, Combination Codes and Personal Identification Codes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777031 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance or assignment

**[[315]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-315 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must not issue a key or assign a combination code or personal
