  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must keep the training
  record for at least two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must make the training
  record available to the Minister on reasonable notice given by the
  Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 30
:::

**\[430 reserved\]**

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Facilitation of Screening]{.HTitleText2} {#h-777770 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777771 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[431]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-431 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
requirements respecting the facilitation of screening operations at an
aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 26
:::

#### [Screening of Passengers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777776 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passenger screening facilities

**[[432]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-432 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for passenger screening
checkpoints and must make at least one facility available for the
private screening of passengers.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 26
- SOR/2014-153, s. 31
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-432-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 432"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False declaration notice

- **[[433]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-433 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must post a notice at each passenger
  screening checkpoint stating that it is an offence for a person at the
  aerodrome to falsely declare

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the person is carrying a weapon, an explosive
    substance, an incendiary device or any other item that could be used
    to jeopardize the security of an aerodrome or aircraft, or that such
    an item is contained in goods in the person's possession or control
    or in goods that the person has tendered or is tendering for
    screening or transportation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that another person who is at an aerodrome or on
    board an aircraft is carrying a weapon, an explosive substance, an
    incendiary device or any other item that could be used to jeopardize
    the security of an aerodrome or aircraft, or that such an item is
    contained in goods in the other person's possession or control or in
    goods that the other person has tendered or is tendering for
    screening or transportation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice must be clearly visible and be in at least
  both official languages.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 26
- SOR/2014-153, s. 31
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-433-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 433"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Notice for Non-passengers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777790 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- Liquids, aerosols or gels

**[[434]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-434 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that non-passengers who access sterile areas are
notified of any restrictions on the possession of liquids, aerosols or
gels in sterile areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 26
:::

#### [Screening of Checked Baggage]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777795 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Checked baggage screening facilities

**[[435]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-435 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must make facilities available for the screening of checked
baggage and baggage intended to be checked baggage.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 26
- SOR/2014-153, s. 32
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-435-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 435"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Baggage Handling Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777800 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No change without agreement

**[[436]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-436 .sectionLabel}** If the operator of an
aerodrome is responsible for a baggage handling system, the operator
must not make any change to the system that may affect screening
operations unless the change is agreed to by CATSA.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 26
:::

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Access Controls]{.HTitleText2} {#h-777805 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777806 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[437]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-437 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the regulatory framework for the protection of security-sensitive areas
of aerodromes.

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777809 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sign requirements

- **[[438]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-438 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must post signs on the outside of each
  restricted area access point and each security barrier. Each sign must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in at least both official languages;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the restricted area as a restricted area;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} state that access to the area is restricted to
    authorized persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signs on security barriers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The signs posted on a security barrier must be no
  more than 150 m apart.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 27
:::

#### [Restricted Area Access Points]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777820 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access control system

**[[439]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-439 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that each restricted area access point that allows
access from a non-restricted area to a restricted area has an access
control system consisting of one or more of the following elements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} surveillance by a person authorized by the operator
  of the aerodrome to control access to restricted area;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} manual locking equipment; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} automated access control equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 28
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passenger loading bridge

**[[440]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-440 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must ensure that each restricted area access point that is
located between an air terminal building and a passenger loading bridge
has a door that can be locked.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 28
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[441]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-441 .sectionLabel}** A person must not enter
a restricted area at an aerodrome except through a restricted area
access point.

#### [Baggage Handling Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777834 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prevention of unauthorized access

**[[442]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-442 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must take measures to prevent unauthorized access to a baggage
handling system that is in a restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 29
:::

#### [Doors, Gates, Emergency Exits and Other Devices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777839 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to close and lock --- operators

- **[[443]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-443 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must close and lock any door, gate or other
  device, other than an emergency exit, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator has control of and responsibility for
    the door, gate or other device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door, gate or other device allows access
    between a restricted area and a non-restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exit system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must institute a system,
  on or near an emergency exit, that prevents access by unauthorized
  persons to a restricted area, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator has control of and responsibility for
    the emergency exit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emergency exit allows access between a
    restricted area and a non-restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to close and lock --- partners and
lessees

- **[[444]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-444 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  primary security line partner, or a lessee other than a primary
  security line partner, at an aerodrome must close and lock any door,
  gate or other device, other than an emergency exit, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the partner or lessee has control of and
    responsibility for the door, gate or other device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door, gate or other device allows access
    between a restricted area and a non-restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exit system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A primary security line partner who occupies an area
  on an aerodrome's primary security line must institute a system, on or
  near an emergency exit, that prevents access by unauthorized persons
  to a restricted area, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the partner has control of and responsibility for
    the emergency exit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emergency exit allows access between a
    restricted area and a non-restricted area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary use or control

**[[445]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-445 .sectionLabel}** Any person at an
aerodrome who has temporary use or control of a door, gate or other
device that allows access between a restricted area and a non-restricted
area must prevent access to or from the restricted area by unauthorized
persons.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Uncontrolled restricted area access point

**[[446]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-446 .sectionLabel}** Unless an authorized
person is controlling access between a restricted area and a
non-restricted area at an aerodrome, a person who enters or leaves the
restricted area must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} lock the door, gate or other device that allows
  access to or from the restricted area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent access to or from the restricted area by
  unauthorized persons while the door, gate or other device is open or
  unlocked.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preventing locking

**[[447]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-447 .sectionLabel}** A person at an
aerodrome must not prevent a door, gate or other device, other than an
emergency exit, that allows access between a restricted area and a
non-restricted area from being locked.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency exits

**[[448]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-448 .sectionLabel}** A person at an
aerodrome must not open any door that is designated as an emergency exit
and that is also a restricted area access point unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is authorized by the operator of the
  aerodrome to open it; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is an emergency.

#### [Unauthorized Access]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777870 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[449]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-449 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person has been given notice, orally, in writing or by a sign, that
  access to a part of an aerodrome is prohibited or is limited to
  authorized persons, the person must not enter or remain in that part
  of the aerodrome without authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restricted areas

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may authorize a person
  to enter or remain in a restricted area if the requirements of
  Divisions 6 and 7 are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public areas other than restricted
  areas

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may authorize a person
  to enter or remain in a part of the aerodrome that is not a public
  area but is not a restricted area if the safety of the aerodrome,
  persons at the aerodrome and aircraft is not jeopardized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public areas other than restricted
  areas

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A lessee at an aerodrome who has the use of, or is
  responsible for, a part of the aerodrome that is not a public area but
  is not a restricted area may authorize a person to enter or remain in
  that part of the aerodrome if the safety of the aerodrome, persons at
  the aerodrome and aircraft is not jeopardized.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 33
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-449-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 449"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Inspectors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777882 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to allow access

**[[450]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-450 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must allow an inspector to enter or remain in a restricted
area if the inspector is acting in the course of their employment and
presents their credentials.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 30
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Documents of Entitlement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-777887 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[451]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-451 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
provisions respecting documents of entitlement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 31
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of documents

- **[[452]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Only
  the following documents are documents of entitlement at an aerodrome:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a restricted area pass;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} a restricted area identity card;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a boarding pass, a ticket, or any other document
    accepted by an air carrier that confirms the status of the person to
    whom it was issued as a passenger on a flight and that is approved
    by the operator of the aerodrome;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a passenger escort form that is approved by the
    operator of the aerodrome;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a courtesy-lounge or conference-room pass that is
    issued by an air carrier and that is approved by the operator of the
    aerodrome; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a document that is issued or approved by the
    operator of the aerodrome in accordance with a security measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pilot's licence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A pilot's licence issued under the [Canadian Aviation
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-96-433) is a document of entitlement
  for a restricted area that is used by general aviation, if the holder
  of the licence also holds a valid medical certificate of a category
  that is appropriate for that licence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is acting in the course of their employment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requires access to an aircraft that they own or
    operate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 31
- SOR/2014-153, s. 34
- SOR/2016-39, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-452-20140613.html "Link to previous version of section 452"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 7.1]{.HLabel2}[Enhanced Access Controls]{.HTitleText2} {#h-777907 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777908 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[452.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.01 .sectionLabel}** This Division
sets out enhanced access control requirements, including requirements
respecting the identity verification system referred to in section 56.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Identity Verification System]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777913 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information

- **[[452.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome is
  authorized to disclose to the Minister or CATSA any information that
  is necessary for the proper operation of the identity verification
  system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the operator of an aerodrome
  must not disclose to CATSA the identity of an applicant for a
  restricted area identity card or the identity of a person to whom a
  restricted area identity card has been issued unless the operator
  grants CATSA access to its databases to maintain or repair the
  identity verification system and CATSA's access to the person's
  identity is incidental to the maintenance or repairs.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Information To Be Displayed on a Restricted Area Identity Card]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777921 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required information

- **[[452.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.03
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must
  ensure that the following information is displayed on each restricted
  area identity card that it issues:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the full name of the person to whom the card is
    issued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 23\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a photograph depicting a frontal view of the
    person's face;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the expiry date of the card;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the aerodrome where the card is issued;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person's employer, if the person
    has a single employer;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the terms "multi-employer" and "employeur
    multiple", if the person has more than one employer.

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 23\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 23\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiration date

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A restricted area identity card, including one that
  is issued to a person who requires access to restricted areas at more
  than one aerodrome, expires no later than five years after the day on
  which it is issued or on the day on which the security clearance of
  the person to whom the card is issued expires, whichever is earlier.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-92, s. 23\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must ensure that all
  information that is displayed on a restricted area identity card is in
  both official languages.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 23](#1343993-1344948){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343993-1344948.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-452.03-20160401.html "Link to previous version of section 452.03"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Issuance of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-777942 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance criteria

- **[[452.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must
  not issue a restricted area identity card to a person unless the
  person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applies in writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is sponsored in writing by their employer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has a security clearance;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} consents in writing to the collection, use,
    retention, disclosure and destruction of information for the
    purposes of this Division; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} confirms that the information displayed on the card
    is correct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Activation requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must not issue a
  restricted area identity card to a person unless the card has been
  activated.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False information

**[[452.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.05 .sectionLabel}** A person must not
provide false information for the purpose of obtaining a restricted area
identity card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sponsorship

**[[452.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.06 .sectionLabel}** An employer must
not

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} sponsor an employee who does not require ongoing
  access to restricted areas in the course of their employment; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} knowingly sponsor an employee for more than one
  restricted area identity card at a time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of multiple cards

**[[452.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.07 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must not issue more than one restricted area identity card
at a time to a person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement of cards

**[[452.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.08 .sectionLabel}** Before replacing
a lost, stolen or non-functional restricted area identity card, the
operator of an aerodrome must ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person applying for the replacement card is the
  person to whom the lost, stolen or non-functional card has been
  issued; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person still has a security clearance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to inform

**[[452.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.09 .sectionLabel}** Before collecting
information from an applicant under this Division, the operator of an
aerodrome must bring to the applicant's attention the purposes for which
the information is collected and the manner in which the information
will be used, retained, disclosed and destroyed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection of information

- **[[452.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of creating a
  restricted area identity card for an applicant, the operator of an
  aerodrome must collect the following information from the applicant:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's full name;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's height;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a photograph depicting a frontal view of the
    applicant's face;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's fingerprint images and iris images;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the applicant's employer; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's occupation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of images and templates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must, immediately after
  issuing the restricted area identity card, destroy all fingerprint
  images and iris images that the operator collected from the applicant
  and any biometric template created from those images that is not
  stored on the card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quality control

**[[452.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.11 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose
of allowing CATSA to monitor the quality of biometric templates and
determining if a restricted area identity card is already active in
respect of an applicant, the operator of an aerodrome must, before
issuing the card, disclose to CATSA any biometric templates created from
the fingerprint images and iris images collected from the applicant.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information

**[[452.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.12 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must take appropriate measures to protect information that
is collected, used, retained or disclosed in accordance with this
Division from loss or theft and from unauthorized access, use,
disclosure, duplication or alteration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Deactivation of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778000 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deactivation request

- **[[452.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome who has
  issued a restricted area identity card must immediately ask CATSA to
  deactivate the card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card expires;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom the card has been issued or
    their employer informs the operator that the card is lost, stolen or
    no longer functional; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom the card has been issued fails,
    on demand, to present or surrender the card to a screening officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reason for deactivation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of an aerodrome asks CATSA to
  deactivate a restricted area identity card, the operator must inform
  CATSA of the reason for the request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must not ask CATSA to
  deactivate a restricted area identity card for a reason other than a
  reason set out in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must notify the Minister
  if the operator asks CATSA to deactivate a restricted area identity
  card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in employment

**[[452.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.14 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome who has issued a restricted area identity card must notify
the Minister immediately if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has a single employer,
  the person to whom the card has been issued ceases to be employed or
  no longer requires ongoing access to restricted areas in the course of
  their employment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has more than one
  employer, the person to whom the card has been issued ceases to be
  employed by all of their employers or no longer requires ongoing
  access to restricted areas in the course of their employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of employer

**[[452.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.15 .sectionLabel}** The employer of a
person to whom a restricted area identity card has been issued must
immediately notify the operator of an aerodrome who issued the card if
the person ceases to be an employee or no longer requires ongoing access
to restricted areas in the course of their employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retrieval of cards

- **[[452.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome who has
  issued a restricted area identity card must take reasonable steps to
  retrieve the card if it has been deactivated and must notify CATSA if
  the card is not retrieved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of cards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a restricted area identity card has been
  deactivated, the person to whom the card has been issued must
  immediately return it to the operator of an aerodrome who issued it
  unless the card was surrendered in accordance with this Division or
  was lost or stolen.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of cards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must, as soon as
  feasible, destroy a restricted area identity card that has been
  retrieved or returned.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 24](#1343999-1344951){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343999-1344951.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-452.16-20160401.html "Link to previous version of section 452.16"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Keys, Combination Codes and Personal Identification Codes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778032 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance or assignment

**[[452.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.17 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must not issue a key or assign a combination code or
personal identification code to a person for a restricted area unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is a person to whom a restricted area
  identity card has been issued and the card is active; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person is in possession of a document that is
  issued or approved by the operator of the aerodrome in accordance with
  a security measure as authorization for the person to enter or remain
  in the restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Addition of key

**[[452.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.18 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome may add a key to a restricted area identity card only if it
is possible to cancel or remove the key without damaging or altering any
other elements of the card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information

**[[452.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.19 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must not add to or modify a restricted area identity card
in any way that might allow the disclosure to CATSA of information about
the person to whom the card has been issued.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation, withdrawal or retrieval

**[[452.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.2 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must cancel, withdraw or retrieve a key that has been issued
to a person who has been issued a restricted area identity card, or a
combination code or personal identification code that has been assigned
to that person, if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person's restricted area identity card has been
  deactivated; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person no longer requires ongoing access to the
  restricted area in the course of their employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Records]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778053 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General requirement

- **[[452.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome and any
  person designated by the operator to issue restricted area identity
  cards or keys or to assign combination codes or personal
  identification codes must keep updated records at the aerodrome
  respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards and keys that have
    been issued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the names of the persons to whom restricted area
    identity cards or keys have been issued;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the names of the persons to whom combination codes
    or personal identification codes have been assigned;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} blank restricted area identity cards in the
    operator's possession;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    deactivated;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} keys, combination codes or personal identification
    codes that have been cancelled, withdrawn or retrieved;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} deactivated restricted area identity cards that
    have not been retrieved by the operator;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    reported as lost or stolen;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} steps taken to retrieve deactivated restricted area
    identity cards;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} compliance with section 452.09; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} restricted area identity cards that have been
    destroyed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deactivated cards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a record respecting a
  restricted area identity card that has been deactivated must be
  retained for at least one year from the day on which the card was
  deactivated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lost or stolen cards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A record respecting a restricted area identity card
  that has been reported as lost or stolen must be retained for at least
  one year from the card's expiry date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision to Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must provide the
  Minister with the records on reasonable notice given by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 25](#1344001-1344955){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1344001-1344955.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-452.21-20160401.html "Link to previous version of section 452.21"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Restricted Area Access Control Process]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778075 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of identity verification system

**[[452.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.22 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must implement and maintain a restricted area access
control process that uses the identity verification system.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Control of Access to Restricted Areas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778080 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unauthorized access prohibition

**[[452.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.23 .sectionLabel}** A person must not
enter or remain in a restricted area unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a person to whom a restricted area identity card
  has been issued; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is in possession of a document of entitlement, other
  than a restricted area identity card, for the restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restricted area identity cards ---
conditions of use

- **[[452.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom a restricted area
  identity card has been issued must not enter or remain in a restricted
  area unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are acting in the course of their employment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the card is in their possession;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the card is active; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} as applicable, they are in possession of a key that
    has been issued to them for the restricted area, or a combination
    code or personal identification code that has been assigned to them
    for the restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(d) does not apply to crew members.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Display of restricted area identity cards

- **[[452.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom a restricted area
  identity card has been issued must not enter or remain in a restricted
  area unless they visibly display the card on their outer clothing at
  all times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Display of temporary passes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom a temporary pass has been issued
  must not enter or remain in a restricted area unless they visibly
  display the pass on their outer clothing at all times.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oversight

**[[452.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.26 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must ensure that a person is not allowed to enter or remain
in a restricted area at the aerodrome unless the person is in possession
of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an active restricted area identity card that has been
  issued to the person; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a document of entitlement, other than a restricted
  area identity card, for the restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Business Continuity Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778111 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Business continuity plans

- **[[452.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must
  develop and maintain a business continuity plan that, at a minimum,
  sets out how the operator will re-establish normal operations and
  comply with section 452.26 in the event that the operator is unable to
  use its restricted area access control process to comply with that
  section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must implement its
  business continuity plan and immediately notify the Minister and CATSA
  if the operator discovers that it is unable to use its restricted area
  access control process to comply with section 452.26.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of delay

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must immediately notify
  the Minister if the operator discovers that it will be unable, for
  more than 24 hours, to use its restricted area access control process
  to comply with section 452.26.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must make its business
  continuity plan available to the Minister on reasonable notice given
  by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 26](#1344003-1344958){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1344003-1344958.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-452.27-20160401.html "Link to previous version of section 452.27"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Database backup

**[[452.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.28 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must regularly back up any database that the operator uses
as part of the identity verification system.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Use of Restricted Area Identity Cards, Keys, Combination Codes and Personal Identification Codes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778127 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General prohibitions

- **[[452.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.29
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person must not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} lend or give a restricted area identity card or a
    key that has been issued to them to another person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use a restricted area identity card or a key that
    has been issued to them to allow access to a restricted area at an
    aerodrome to another person without authorization from the operator
    of the aerodrome;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} intentionally alter or otherwise modify a
    restricted area identity card or a key unless they are the operator
    of an aerodrome or a person designated by the operator;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} use a restricted area identity card or a key that
    has been issued to another person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} have in their possession, without reasonable
    excuse, a restricted area identity card or a key that has been
    issued to another person;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} use a counterfeit restricted area identity card or
    a counterfeit key; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} make a copy of a restricted area identity card or a
    key.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure or use of codes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person, other than the operator of an aerodrome or
  a person designated by the operator, must not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} disclose a combination code or personal
    identification code; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use another person's combination code or personal
    identification code.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of loss or theft

- **[[452.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom a restricted area
  identity card or a key has been issued must immediately report its
  loss or theft to their employer or to the operator of an aerodrome who
  issued the card or key.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's duty to report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer who is informed by an employee of the
  loss or theft of a restricted area identity card or a key must
  immediately report the loss or theft to the operator of an aerodrome
  who issued the card or key.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of non-functioning card

**[[452.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.31 .sectionLabel}** An employer who
is informed by an employee that a restricted area identity card is not
functioning must immediately notify the operator of an aerodrome who
issued the card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Presentation and Surrender of Restricted Area Identity Cards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778155 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation on demand

- **[[452.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person in possession of a
  restricted area identity card who is in a restricted area at an
  aerodrome must, on demand, present the card to the Minister, the
  operator of the aerodrome, the person's employer or a peace officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation during screening

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person in possession of a restricted area identity
  card who is being screened by a screening officer at a restricted area
  access point or at a location in a restricted area must, on demand,
  present the card to the screening officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surrender on demand

- **[[452.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person in possession of a
  restricted area identity card must, on demand, surrender it to the
  Minister, the operator of an aerodrome, a screening officer or a peace
  officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand by Minister or operator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister or the operator of an aerodrome may
  demand the surrender of a restricted area identity card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card has expired or has been reported as lost
    or stolen;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the card has been deactivated; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the surrender of the card is required to ensure
    aviation security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand by screening officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A screening officer may demand the surrender of a
  restricted area identity card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card has expired or has been reported as lost
    or stolen;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the card has been deactivated; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the screening officer is carrying out screening at
    a restricted area access point or at a location in a restricted area
    and the person who is in possession of the card refuses to be
    screened or refuses to submit goods in their possession or control
    for screening.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand by peace officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A peace officer may demand the surrender of a
  restricted area identity card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the card has expired or has been reported as lost
    or stolen; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is an immediate threat to aviation security,
    the security of any aircraft or aerodrome or other aviation facility
    or the safety of the public, passengers or crew members, and the
    surrender of the card is required to respond to the threat.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of cards

**[[452.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.34 .sectionLabel}** A screening
officer or a peace officer to whom a person surrenders a restricted area
identity card must return the card to the operator of the aerodrome
where the card is surrendered or to the operator of an aerodrome who
issued the card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of Minister

**[[452.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.35 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome to whom a person surrenders a restricted area identity card
must notify the Minister if the operator demanded the surrender in
accordance with paragraph 452.33(2)(c).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Escort and Surveillance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778190 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General requirement

- **[[452.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must
  ensure that any person who is in a restricted area at the aerodrome
  and is not in possession of a restricted area identity card

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is escorted by a person in possession of an active
    restricted area identity card that has been issued to them; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is kept under surveillance by a person in
    possession of an active restricted area identity card that has been
    issued to them, in the case of an area the limits of which are
    defined for a specific purpose, such as construction or maintenance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of the
  following persons:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} passengers who have been screened; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} inspectors.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Escort ratio

- **[[452.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must
  ensure that at least one escort is provided for every 10 persons who
  require escort.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surveillance ratio

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome must ensure that no more
  than 20 persons at a time are kept under surveillance by one person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to remain together

- **[[452.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person under escort must remain
  with the escort while the person is in a restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An escort must remain with the person under escort
  while the person is in a restricted area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to inform

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The person who appoints an escort must inform the
  escort of the requirement to remain with the person under escort while
  that person is in a restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Screening requirement

**[[452.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.39 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must ensure that a person under escort or surveillance at
the aerodrome and any goods in the person's possession or control are
screened at a screening checkpoint before the person enters a sterile
area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- conveyances

- **[[452.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome is not
  required to place an escort or surveillance personnel in a conveyance
  that is in a restricted area at the aerodrome and is carrying persons
  who require escort or surveillance if the conveyance travels in a
  convoy with an escort conveyance that contains at least one person in
  possession of an active restricted area identity card that has been
  issued to them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception to exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must ensure that, if a
  person who requires escort or surveillance disembarks from a
  conveyance in a restricted area at the aerodrome, the person is
  escorted or kept under surveillance in accordance with section 452.37.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Escort conveyances

**[[452.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.41 .sectionLabel}** The operator of
an aerodrome must ensure that, at the aerodrome, at least one escort
conveyance is provided for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} every three conveyances requiring escort to or from
  an air terminal building apron area for a purpose other than snow
  removal operations;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} every six conveyances requiring escort to or from an
  air terminal building apron area for snow removal operations; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} every six conveyances requiring escort to or from a
  restricted area other than an air terminal building apron area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

#### [Inspectors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778236 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

**[[452.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.42 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Division requires an inspector acting in the course of their employment
to be in possession of a restricted area identity card or any other
document issued or approved by the operator of an aerodrome as
authorization for the inspector to enter or remain in a restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspector's credentials

**[[452.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.43 .sectionLabel}** The credentials
issued by the Minister to an inspector do not constitute a restricted
area identity card even if the credentials are compatible with the
identity verification system or with an access control system
established by the operator of an aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Escort privileges

**[[452.44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.44 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Division prohibits an inspector from escorting a person who is in a
restricted area and is not in possession of a restricted area identity
card if the inspector

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is acting in the course of their employment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not escort more than 10 persons at one time;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} remains with the person while the person is in the
  restricted area;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensures that the person remains with the inspector
  while the person is in the restricted area; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} ensures that the person and any goods in their
  possession or control are screened at a screening checkpoint before
  the person enters a sterile area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conveyance escort privileges

- **[[452.45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-452.45
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Division prohibits
  an inspector from escorting a person who is in a conveyance in a
  restricted area and is not in possession of a restricted area identity
  card if the inspector

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is acting in the course of their employment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not escort more than 10 persons at one time;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is either in the conveyance or in an escort
    conveyance that is travelling in a convoy with the conveyance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person under escort disembarks from a conveyance
  in a restricted area, the inspector must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} remain with the person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the person remains with the inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person under escort is travelling to or from an
  air terminal building apron area, the Minister must ensure that at
  least one escort conveyance is provided for every three conveyances
  requiring escort in a convoy and that at least one inspector is in
  each escort conveyance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a person under escort is travelling to or from a
  restricted area other than an air terminal building apron area, the
  Minister must ensure that at least one escort conveyance is provided
  for every six conveyances requiring escort in a convoy and that at
  least one inspector is in each escort conveyance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-39, s. 3
:::

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Airport Security Programs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-778270 .Subheading}

#### [Overview]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778271 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division overview

**[[453]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-453 .sectionLabel}** This Division sets out
the regulatory framework for promoting a comprehensive, coordinated and
integrated approach to airport security. The processes required under
this Division are intended to facilitate the establishment and
implementation of effective airport security programs that reflect the
circumstances of each aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-453-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 453"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778276 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Processes and procedures

**[[454]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-454 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
any reference to a process in this Division includes the procedures, if
any, that are necessary to implement that process.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-454-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 454"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Airport Security Program Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778281 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to establish and implement

- **[[455]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-455 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must establish and implement an airport
  security program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As part of its airport security program, the operator
  of an aerodrome must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} define and document the aerodrome-related security
    roles and responsibilities assigned to each of the operator's
    employee groups and contractor groups;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicate the information referred to in
    paragraph (a) to the employees and contractors in those groups;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have a security policy statement that establishes
    an overall commitment and direction for aerodrome security and sets
    out the operator's security objectives;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} communicate the security policy statement in an
    accessible manner to all persons who are employed at the aerodrome
    or who require access to the aerodrome in the course of their
    employment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a process for responding to
    aerodrome-related security incidents and breaches in a coordinated
    manner that is intended to minimize their impact;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a security awareness
    program that promotes a culture of security vigilance and awareness
    among the following persons:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} persons who are employed at the aerodrome,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} crew members who are based at the aerodrome, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} persons, other than crew members, who require
      access to the aerodrome in the course of their employment;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} assess risk information and disseminate it within
    the operator's organization for the purpose of informed
    decision-making about aviation security;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} establish and implement a process for receiving,
    retaining, disclosing and disposing of sensitive information
    respecting aviation security in order to protect the information
    from unauthorized access;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} identify sensitive information respecting aviation
    security and receive, retain, disclose and dispose of sensitive
    information respecting aviation security in a manner that protects
    the information from unauthorized access;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} disclose sensitive information respecting aviation
    security to the following persons if they have been assigned
    aerodrome-related security roles and responsibilities and require
    the information to carry out those roles and responsibilities:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} persons who are employed at the aerodrome, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} persons who require access to the aerodrome in
      the course of their employment;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} have a current scale map of the aerodrome that
    identifies all restricted areas, security barriers and restricted
    area access points; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} document how the operator achieves compliance with
    the aviation security provisions of the Act and the regulatory
    requirements that apply to the operator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other program requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following also form part of the airport security
  program:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the security official referred to in section 425;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the aerodrome security personnel training referred
    to in sections 426 and 427;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the security committee or other working group or
    forum referred to in section 458;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the airport security risk assessment
    referred to in section 461;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the strategic airport security plan
    referred to in section 466;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the menu of additional safeguards referred to in
    section 472;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the emergency plan referred to in section 474; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the security exercises referred to in sections 475
    and 476.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, ss. 32, 65(F)
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-455-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 455"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

- **[[456]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-456 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} keep documentation related to its menu of
    additional safeguards and any amendment to it for at least five
    years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, keep documentation related to its
    airport security risk assessment and any review of it for at least
    five years;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, keep documentation related to its
    strategic airport security plan and any amendment to it for at least
    five years; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} keep all other documentation related to its airport
    security program for at least two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial access

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must make the
  documentation available to the Minister on reasonable notice given by
  the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-456-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 456"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to amend

**[[457]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-457 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must amend its airport security program if the operator
identifies, at the aerodrome, an aviation security risk that is not
addressed by the program.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-457-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 457"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Committee]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778331 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security committee

- **[[458]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-458 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must have a security committee or other
  working group or forum that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} advises the operator on the development of controls
    and processes that are necessary at the aerodrome in order to comply
    with the aviation security provisions of the Act and the regulatory
    requirements that apply to the operator;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} helps coordinate the implementation of the controls
    and processes that are necessary at the aerodrome in order to comply
    with the aviation security provisions of the Act and the regulatory
    requirements that apply to the operator; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} promotes the sharing of information respecting the
    airport security program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must manage the
  security committee or other working group or forum in accordance with
  written terms of reference that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} identify its membership; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} define the roles and responsibilities of each
    member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must keep records of
  the activities and decisions of the security committee or other
  working group or forum.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-458-20120101.html "Link to previous version of section 458"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Requirements that Apply only if an Amendment to Schedule 3 or a Ministerial Order is made: Airport Security Risk Assessments and Strategic Airport Security Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778346 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[459]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-459
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 460, sections 461
  to 471 apply to the operator of an aerodrome if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Governor in Council makes an aviation security
    regulation adding an asterisk in Schedule 3 after the International
    Civil Aviation Organization (ICAO) location indicator of the
    aerodrome; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister makes an order stating that
    sections 461 to 471 apply to the operator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's authority

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister is authorized to make orders stating
  that sections 461 to 471 apply to operators of aerodromes listed in
  Schedule 3.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 39](#1344031-1345074){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1344031-1345074.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-459-20140613.html "Link to previous version of section 459"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transition

- **[[460]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-460
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 461 and 462 do not apply to
  the operator of an aerodrome until the day that is 10 months after the
  earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which an aviation security regulation
    adding an asterisk in Schedule 3 after the International Civil
    Aviation Organization (ICAO) location indicator of the aerodrome
    comes into force, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which a ministerial order stating that
    sections 461 to 471 apply to the operator comes into force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 466 and 468 do not apply to the operator of
  an aerodrome until the day that is 22 months after the earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which an aviation security regulation
    adding an asterisk in Schedule 3 after the International Civil
    Aviation Organization (ICAO) location indicator of the aerodrome
    comes into force, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which a ministerial order stating that
    sections 461 to 471 apply to the operator comes into force.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
- [SOR/2022-92, s. 39](#1344031-1345077){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1344031-1345077.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-460-20140613.html "Link to previous version of section 460"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Airport security risk assessments

**[[461]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-461 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must have an airport security risk assessment that identifies,
assesses and prioritizes aviation security risks and that includes the
following elements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a threat assessment that evaluates the probability
  that aviation security incidents will occur at the aerodrome;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a criticality assessment that prioritizes the areas,
  assets, infrastructure and operations at or associated with the
  aerodrome that most require protection from acts and attempted acts of
  unlawful interference with civil aviation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a vulnerability assessment that considers the extent
  to which the areas, assets, infrastructure and operations at or
  associated with the aerodrome are susceptible to loss or damage and
  that evaluates this susceptibility in the context of the threat
  assessment; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an impact assessment that, at a minimum, measures the
  consequences of an aviation security incident or potential aviation
  security incident in terms of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a decrease in public safety and security,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} financial and economic loss, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a loss of public confidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission for approval

**[[462]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-462 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must submit its airport security risk assessment to the
Minister for approval, and must submit a new airport security risk
assessment to the Minister within five years after the date of the most
recent approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to consult

**[[463]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-463 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must consult its security committee or other working group or
forum when the operator is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} preparing its airport security risk assessment for
  submission to the Minister for approval; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} conducting a review of its airport security risk
  assessment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Airport security risk assessment ---
annual review

- **[[464]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-464 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must conduct a review of its airport security
  risk assessment at least once a year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Airport security risk assessment ---
  other reviews

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must also conduct a
  review of its airport security risk assessment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a special event that is scheduled to take place at
    the aerodrome could affect aerodrome security;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator is planning a change to the physical
    layout or operation of the aerodrome that could affect aviation
    security at the aerodrome;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an environmental or operational change at the
    aerodrome could affect aerodrome security;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a change in regulatory requirements could affect
    aerodrome security;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operator identifies, at the aerodrome, a
    vulnerability that is not addressed in the assessment, or the
    Minister identifies such a vulnerability to the operator; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that there is a
    change in the threat environment that could result in a new or
    unaddressed medium to high risk.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equivalency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a review conducted under
  subsection (2) counts as a review required under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When the operator of the aerodrome conducts a review
  of its airport security risk assessment, the operator must document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any decision to amend or to not amend the
    assessment or the operator's risk-management strategy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for that decision; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the factors that were taken into consideration in
    making that decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator of the aerodrome must notify the
  Minister if, as a result of a review of its airport security risk
  assessment, the operator amends the assessment

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to include a new medium to high risk; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to raise or lower the level of a risk within the
    medium to high range.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

**[[465]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-465 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must
approve an airport security risk assessment submitted by the operator of
an aerodrome if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assessment meets the requirements of section 461;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the assessment has been reviewed by an executive
  within the operator's organization who is responsible for security;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operator has considered the advice of its
  security committee or other working group or forum;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the operator has considered all available and
  relevant information; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operator has not overlooked an aviation security
  risk that could affect the operation of the aerodrome.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Strategic airport security plans

**[[466]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-466 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must establish a strategic airport security plan that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} summarizes the operator's strategy to prepare for,
  detect, prevent, respond to and recover from acts or attempted acts of
  unlawful interference with civil aviation; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes a risk-management strategy that addresses
  the medium to high aviation security risks identified and prioritized
  in the operator's airport security risk assessment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to consult

**[[467]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-467 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must consult its security committee or other working group or
forum when establishing its strategic airport security plan.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to submit

**[[468]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-468 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must submit its strategic airport security plan to the
Minister for approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to implement

**[[469]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-469 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must, as soon as its strategic airport security plan is
approved, implement its risk-management strategy.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of plan

**[[470]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-470 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must
approve a strategic airport security plan submitted by the operator of
an aerodrome if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plan meets the requirements of section 466;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the plan has been reviewed by an executive within the
  operator's organization who is responsible for security;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the plan is likely to enable the operator to prepare
  for, detect, prevent, respond to and recover from acts or attempted
  acts of unlawful interference with civil aviation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the risk-management strategy is in proportion to the
  risks it addresses;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operator has considered the advice of its
  security committee or other working group or forum;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the operator has not overlooked an aviation security
  risk that could affect the operation of the aerodrome; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the plan can be implemented without compromising
  aviation security.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments

- **[[471]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-471 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome may amend its strategic airport security plan
  at any time, but must do so if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plan does not reflect the operator's airport
    security risk assessment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that there is a
    change in the threat environment that could result in a new or
    unaddressed medium to high risk;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that its
    risk-management strategy is not in proportion to a medium to high
    risk set out in the operator's airport security risk assessment; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the operator identifies a deficiency in the plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation --- risk-management
  strategy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its
  risk-management strategy, the operator must document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reason for the amendment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the factors that were taken into consideration in
    making that amendment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation --- strategic airport
  security plan

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its strategic
  airport security plan, the operator must document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reason for the amendment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the factors that were taken into consideration in
    making that amendment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of amendment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its strategic
  airport security plan, the operator must, as soon as possible, submit
  the amendment to the Minister for approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must approve an amendment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an amendment to the summary required
    under paragraph 466(a), the conditions set out in paragraphs 470(a)
    to (c) have been met; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an amendment to the risk-management
    strategy required under paragraph 466(b), the conditions set out in
    section 470 have been met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its
  risk-management strategy, the operator must implement the amended
  version of the strategy once it is approved by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

#### [Menu of Additional Safeguards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778475 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to establish

- **[[472]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-472 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must establish a menu of additional
  safeguards that are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} intended to mitigate heightened risk conditions in
    a graduated manner; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} consistent with the operator's legal powers and
    obligations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Menu requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The menu of additional safeguards must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} describe, by activity type and location, the
    safeguards in place at the aerodrome in respect of AVSEC level 1
    operating conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow the rapid selection of additional safeguards
    by activity type or location; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} indicate the persons and organizations responsible
    for implementing each additional safeguard.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Activity types

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (2)(a) and (b), the
  activity types must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} access controls;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} monitoring and patrolling;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} communications; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} other operational controls.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Locations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (2)(a) and (b), the
  locations must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} public areas of the aerodrome;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} areas of the aerodrome that are not public areas
    but are not restricted areas; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} restricted areas.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to submit

**[[473]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-473 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must submit its menu of additional safeguards to the Minister
for approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

**[[473.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-473.1 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must
approve a menu of additional safeguards submitted by the operator of an
aerodrome if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the menu meets the requirements of section 472;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the menu has been reviewed by an executive within the
  operator's organization who is responsible for security;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the additional safeguards can be implemented rapidly
  and consistently;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the additional safeguards are consistent with
  existing rights and freedoms; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the additional safeguards can be implemented without
  compromising aviation security.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments

- **[[473.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-473.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an aerodrome may
  amend its menu of additional safeguards at any time, but must do so if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister informs the operator that there is a
    change in the threat environment that requires the addition or
    deletion of additional safeguards;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator identifies a deficiency in the menu;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a change is made in the aviation security
    provisions of the Act or in regulatory requirements and the change
    affects the additional safeguards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of amendment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the operator of the aerodrome amends its menu of
  additional safeguards, the operator must, as soon as possible, submit
  the amendment to the Minister for approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must approve the amendment if the
  conditions set out in section 473.1 continue to be met.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

#### [Emergency Plans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778524 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plan requirements

- **[[474]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-474 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must establish an emergency plan that sets
  out the response procedures to be followed at the aerodrome for
  coordinated responses to the following emergencies:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} bomb threats;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} hijackings of aircraft; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} other acts of unlawful interference with civil
    aviation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Response procedures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The response procedures must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out in detail the actions to be taken by the
    employees and contractors of the operator of the aerodrome and
    identify the responsibilities of all other persons or organizations
    involved, including, as applicable, the police, emergency response
    providers, air carriers, emergency coordination centre personnel and
    control tower or flight service station personnel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the evacuation of
    air terminal buildings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the search of air
    terminal buildings;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the handling and
    disposal of a suspected bomb; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} include detailed procedures for the detention on
    the ground of any aircraft involved in a bomb threat or hijacking.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 33
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-474-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 474"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Exercises]{.HTitleText3} {#h-778540 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operations-based security exercise

- **[[475]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-475 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must, at least once every four years, carry
  out an operations-based security exercise that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of the operator's emergency
    plan in response to an act of unlawful interference with civil
    aviation and involves the persons and organizations referred to in
    the plan; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of additional safeguards
    that the operator chooses from its menu of additional safeguards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equivalency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, in response to an aviation security incident, the
  Minister raises the AVSEC level for an aerodrome or any part of an
  aerodrome, the implementation of additional safeguards by the operator
  of the aerodrome counts as an operations-based security exercise for
  the purposes of subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 33
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-475-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 475"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discussion-based security exercise

- **[[476]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-476 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of an aerodrome must, at least once a year, carry out a
  discussion-based security exercise that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of the operator's emergency
    plan in response to an act of unlawful interference with civil
    aviation and involves the persons and organizations referred to in
    the plan; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} tests the effectiveness of additional safeguards
    that the operator chooses from its menu of additional safeguards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the operator of an aerodrome
  is not required to carry out a discussion-based security exercise in
  any year in which it carries out an operations-based security
  exercise.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-48, s. 33
- SOR/2014-153, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-476-20120315.html "Link to previous version of section 476"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

**[[477]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-477 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
aerodrome must give the Minister 60 days' notice of any security
